Global well-posedness and uniform-in-time vanishing damping limit for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model
Xinyu Cheng, Zhaonan Luo, Zhaojie Yang, Cheng Yuan

Abstract
. In this paper, we consider global strong solutions and the uniform-in-time vanishing damping limit for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model in ℝ d \mathbb{R}^{d} , where d = 2 d=2 and 3 3 . The well-recognized problem of the global existence of smooth solutions for the 2D inviscid Oldroyd-B model without smallness assumptions is open due to the complex structure of Q ⁡ ( ∇ u , τ ) ≠ 0 Q(\nabla u,\tau)\neq 0 . Therefore, improving the smallness assumptions, especially in the lower regularity class, is the core question in the area of fluid models. On the other hand, long-time behaviors of solutions including temporal decay and uniform-in-time damping stability are also of deep significance. These problems have been widely studied; however, the existing results are not regularity critical and the (uniform) vanishing damping limit has not been discussed. To tackle these challenges, we first establish local well-posedness in the sense of Hadamard with critical regularity. Then, by virtue of the sharp commutator estimate for Calderon–Zygmund operators, we establish global well-posedness for d = 2 d=2 with damping in the low regularity class ( L 2 ∩ B ∞ , 1 1 ) × ( L 2 ∩ B ∞ , 1 0 ) (L^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times(L^{2}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}) , which, to the best of our knowledge, is novel in the literature. Furthermore, in both the 2D and 3D cases, we prove global existence of solutions to the inviscid Oldroyd-B model uniformly in the damping parameter a ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] a\in[0,1] . In addition, we obtain optimal temporal decay rates and time integrability by improving the existing Fourier splitting method and developing a novel temporal decomposition strategy. These results lead to the major contribution of the present paper: the uniform-in-time vanishing damping limit for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model. More specifically, we discover the correlation between the sharp vanishing damping rate and the temporal decay rate. On periodic domains, we also prove exponential relaxation toward spatially homogeneous equilibria: the nonzero Fourier modes decay at a rate uniform in a ∈ [ 0 , 1 ] a\in[0,1] , while the decay rate of the stress zero mode necessarily degenerates as a → 0 a\to 0 . We support our findings by providing numerical evidence. 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification : 35Q31, 76A05, 74B20, 35B40. Keywords : The inviscid Oldroyd-B models; Global well-posedness; Uniform-in-time vanishing damping limit.



1.
Introduction

In this manuscript, we consider the general Oldroyd-B models. The Oldroyd-B model serves as a constitutive system for describing the flow behavior of viscoelastic fluids, combining both viscous and elastic properties. It extends the upper-convected Maxwell model and accounts for time-dependent stress responses. The model characterizes stress as a combination of a Newtonian viscous component and a viscoelastic component, effectively capturing phenomena including stress relaxation and creep. This makes it particularly useful for studying polymers, biological fluids, and other complex materials.

Oldroyd-B model was first introduced systematically by Oldroyd in the 1950s
[
37
,
38
]
to describe constitutive models for viscoelastic fluids with respect to material frame indifference. To be more specific, in this monograph, we consider the general Oldroyd-B models as follows:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla P={\rm div}~\tau+\nu\Delta u,~~~~{\rm div}~u=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}\tau+u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q(\nabla u,\tau)=\alpha D(u)+\mu\Delta\tau,\\[4.30554pt]
u|_{t=0}=u_{0},~~\tau|_{t=0}=\tau_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

We shall assume that the fluid domain is the entire space
\mathbb{R}^{d}
for
d=2,3
. In (
1
),
u(t,x)
denotes the velocity of the polymeric liquid,
\tau(t,x)
represents the symmetric tensor of constraints, and
P
is the pressure. Moreover, the bilinear term is given as follows:

(1.4)

Q(\nabla u,\tau)=\tau\Omega-\Omega\tau-b(D(u)\tau+\tau D(u)),

where
b\in[-1,1]
, the vorticity tensor is given by
\Omega=\frac{\nabla u-(\nabla u)^{T}}{2}
and the deformation tensor is defined by
D(u)=\frac{\nabla u+(\nabla u)^{T}}{2}
. The viscosity coefficient
\nu
and the diffusion coefficient
\mu
are non-negative. From the derivation of polymer fluids, parameter
\alpha\geq 0
and parameter
b
are related, see
[
15
]
. The constitutive term is co-rotational when
b=0
. The special case
\alpha=b=0
additionally removes the deformation forcing
\alpha D(u)
. In this paper, we focus on the inviscid Oldroyd-B model with
\nu=0
and
\alpha=\mu=1
. Here,
a
represents the damping coefficient and is inversely proportional to the Weissenberg number
W_{e}
, the dimensionless ratio of the polymer relaxation time to a characteristic flow time. The larger the
W_{e}
number, the more significant the elastic effect of the fluid has. The large Weissenberg number problem
[
14
]
is challenging both numerically and analytically.

1.1.
Introduction to the Oldroyd-B models and historical review

To start with, we introduce some results from the existing literature of the Oldroyd-B models.

Taking
\nu>0
and
\mu=0
in (
1
), we obtain the classical Oldroyd-B model. In
[
22
]
, C. Guillopé, and J. C. Saut first proved that the Oldroyd-B model admits a unique global strong solution in Sobolev spaces. For case
b=0
, the weak solutions of the co-rotational Oldroyd-B model were obtained by P. L. Lions and N. Masmoudi
[
35
]
. We also note that the problem for case
b\neq 0
remains open, as discussed in
[
36
]
. J. Y. Chemin and N. Masmoudi
[
9
]
established the existence and uniqueness of strong solutions in homogeneous Besov spaces with critical regularity. Optimal decay rates for solutions to the 3-D Oldroyd-B model were proved by M. Hieber, H. Wen and R. Zi
[
24
]
.

Taking
\nu=0
and
\mu>0
in (
1
), we obtain the inviscid Oldroyd-B model. We will present the relevant mathematical results in two cases.

(1) The case with damping (
a>0
).

For
d=2
, considering the inviscid Oldroyd-B model with initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in H^{s}
for
s>2
, the system (
1.3
) was proved to admit a unique global strong solution
(u,\tau)\in C([0,\infty);H^{s})
in
[
19
]
if

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\omega_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq{\epsilon},~~~~\omega_{0}={\rm curl}~u_{0}

for some sufficiently small
\epsilon
. The large data global solutions under the additional condition
Q=0
can be obtained as well. However, the problem of the global existence of smooth solutions for the general 2D inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) (
Q\neq 0
) without any smallness assumption is still open due to the complex structure of
Q(\nabla u,\tau)
.

Recently, W. Deng, Z. Luo and Z. Yin
[
15
,
16
]
proved global existence of strong solutions and weak solutions to the co-rotational inviscid Oldroyd-B model.

(2) The case without damping (
a=0
).

P. Constantin, J. Wu, J. Zhao and Y. Zhu
[
14
]
established the global well-posedness of the inviscid Oldroyd-B models with small data.

Sharp decay estimates for the Oldroyd-B model with
only fractional stress tensor diffusion were proved in
[
41
]
. We now describe the results for the equation (
1.3
) with integral stress tensor diffusion. Let
d=3
. Considering the inviscid Oldroyd-B model with initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in H^{r}\cap L^{1}
with
r=\frac{25}{2}
, P. Wang, J. Wu, X. Xu and Y. Zhong
[
41
]
proved that there exists some sufficiently small constant
{\epsilon}
such that if

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{r}\cap L^{1}}\leq{\epsilon},

then the system (
1.3
) admits a unique global solution
(u,\tau)
that obeys the following decay properties

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\epsilon}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}},~~~~\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\epsilon}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}},~~~~\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\epsilon}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}},

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim{\epsilon}(1+t)^{-2},~~~~\|\mathbb{P}\rm{div}~\tau\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim{\epsilon}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}},

where
\mathbb{P}
denotes the Leray projection onto divergence-free vector fields.

W. Deng, Z. Luo and Z. Yin
[
16
]
proved optimal decay rate of global weak solutions for
d=2
by the improved Fourier splitting method.

1.2.
Relevant Models

When considering inviscid fluids, the standard model is the Euler equation. The incompressible Euler equation (
1.2
) is useful in modeling scenarios where the fluid behaves more like an ideal fluid.

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla P=0,~~~~{\rm div}~u=0,\\[4.30554pt]
u|_{t=0}=u_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

We now provide relevant references about the Euler equation in the following, which greatly help us understand the behaviour of inviscid fluids. V. I. Yudovich
[
43
]
established the existence and uniqueness of global weak solutions for the Euler equations with
d=2
. J. T. Beale, T. Kato and A. Majda
[
3
]
proved the celebrated BKM criterion for determining the global existence of strong solutions to the Euler equations. A. Kiselev and V. Sverak
[
29
]
obtained a lower bound estimate for the double exponential growth of solutions to the Euler equations with
d=2
. The local well-posedness of the Euler equation with critical regularity was proved in
[
23
]
. T. Hmidi and S. Keraani
[
25
]
proved the rate of vanishing viscosity in borderline Besov spaces. For the surface quasi-geostrophic equations, X. Cheng, Z. Luo and S. Wang
[
12
]
study vanishing viscosity rates and same-topology convergence in borderline Besov spaces. J. Bourgain and D. Li
[
5
]
established strong ill-posedness of the Euler equations in borderline sobolev spaces. T. Buckmaster, C. De Lellis, L. Székelyhidi, Jr., V. Vicol
[
7
]
and P. Isett
[
27
]
independently proved Onsager’s conjecture by constructing non-trivial weak solutions via the convex integration scheme. Recently V.Giri, H. Kwon and M. Novack have proved a
L^{3}
-based Onsager’s conjecture in
[
20
,
21
]
.

For other relevant models, Z. Lei, C. Liu and Y. Zhou
[
31
]
proved the existence of global solutions for incompressible viscoelastic fluids, which is a simplified model of the Oldroyd-B model in a certain case. In
[
30
]
, Z. Lei proved the global well-posedness of incompressible elastodynamics in two dimensions; a similar result was obtained independently by X. Wang in
[
42
]
using a normal form method. The two-dimensional Euler-Poisson system was proved to admit global solutions by Li and Wu in
[
34
]
; global regularity results of the 2D fractional Boussinesq equations are obtained by Stefanov, Wu, Xu and Ye in
[
40
]
.

Moreover, it is also worth mentioning that the non-uniqueness of low regularity solutions to other related fluid equations has been studied widely.
For example, non-uniqueness of forced Navier–Stokes equations has been studied by D. Albritton, E. Brué and M. Colombo
[
1
]
, and non-uniqueness of time-dependent SQG equations has been studied by T. Buckmaster, S. Shkoller and V. Vicol in
[
8
]
. X. Cheng, H. Kwon and D. Li have also shown the non-uniqueness of stationary solutions to the SQG equations in
[
10
]
.

1.3.
The main results

Taking
\alpha=\mu=1
,
\nu=0
in (
1
), then we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla P={\rm div}~\tau,~~~~{\rm div}~u=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}\tau+u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q(\nabla u,\tau)=D(u)+\Delta\tau,\\[4.30554pt]
u|_{t=0}=u_{0},~~\tau|_{t=0}=\tau_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Our main results can be stated as follows.

Theorem 1.1

(low regularity GWP for the
a>0
case)
.

Let
d=2
and
a\in(0,1]
. Assume a divergence-free field
u_{0}\in L^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}
and a symmetric matrix
\tau_{0}\in L^{2}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. There exists some
c=c(a)>0
small enough such that if

(1.10)

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{L^{2}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq c,

then (
1.3
) admits a global solution
(u,\tau)
with

(u,\tau)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}).

Theorem 1.2

(GWP and vanishing damping limit in finite time)
.

Let
d=2,3
and
a\in[0,1]
. Assume a divergence-free field
u_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}
and a symmetric matrix
\tau_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. There exists some positive constant
{\epsilon}
small enough such that if

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq{\epsilon},

then (
1.3
) admits a global solution
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
with

(u^{a},\tau^{a})\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}).

Moreover, for any
T>0
, there exists positive constant
C_{T}
depending on
T
such that the following hold

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}\leq C_{T}a,

and

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Remark 1.3
.

The result of the global existence in Theorem
1.2
extends the research of critical regularity to the case
a\in[0,1]
. In addition, for any
T>0
, we prove vanishing damping limit under the same topology of the initial data.

Remark 1.4
.

The local well-posedness of the Euler equation in critical Besov space
B^{1}_{\infty,1}
was established in
[
23
]
. One can see that the regularity of velocity
u^{a}
in Theorem
1.2
is critical, since
u^{a}
satisfies the Euler equation with force. However, for
a=0
, we need the
H^{1}
estimate of
\tau^{a}
to close this critical estimate. For the case
a>0
, we point out that damping effect is crucial to reducing the regularity of
\tau^{a}
, see Theorem
1.1
.

Remark 1.5
.

By the theory of the transport equation (cf.
[
2
]
), the velocity
u\in C^{0,1}
is the key to persisting regularity. Under the conditions in Theorem
1.2
, we deduce that the global existence of smooth solutions
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
for the equation (
1.3
) with extra smooth initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
.

Remark 1.6
.

In the 3D case, the vorticity equation has an extra
\omega^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
term, therefore the main difference and challenge is to control this term.

Theorem 1.7

(Optimal uniform-in-time vanishing damping limit)
.

Under the same conditions as in Theorem
1.2
, if additionally
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in\dot{B}^{-\frac{d}{2}}_{2,\infty},

then there exists a positive constant
C_{0}
depending on the initial data such that

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{2}}+(1+t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{d}{4}},

and

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\leq C_{0}.

Furthermore, the following sharp uniform-in-time vanishing damping rate holds

(1.11)

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2})}\leq C_{0}a^{\frac{d}{4}},

and

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Remark 1.8
.

For the case
d=2
and
a=0
, optimal time decay rates of global solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) are critical, i.e.
\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{-1}
in the case of high regularity, cf.
[
16
]
. This borderline upper bound alone does not imply time integrability of
\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. We introduce a new method of space-time frequency decomposition to obtain the time integrability of
\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. The key time integrability of
\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
enables us to discover a completely new phenomenon that there is a correlation between the uniform vanishing damping rate and the time decay rate.

Remark 1.9
.

The uniform-in-time
L^{2}
vanishing damping rate (
1.11
) is sharp in the sense that we can find initial data satisfying our condition such that

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2})}\gtrsim a^{\frac{d}{4}}.

We refer the readers to Propositions
5.9
and
6.5
for more discussion, where we will present examples satisfying the exact vanishing damping rate.

Remark 1.10
.

In the case with
d=3
and high regularity, optimal time decay rates of global solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B models were proved in
[
41
]
. We generalize the results to the critical regularity case by proving the key integrability of
\|(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
, which also helps to discover that there is a relationship between uniform vanishing damping rate and time decay rate.

1.4.
Motivations, difficulties and main ideas

The well-recognized problem of the global existence of smooth solutions for the 2D inviscid Oldroyd-B model without smallness assumptions is open due to the complex structure of
Q(\nabla u,\tau)\neq 0
. Therefore improving the smallness assumptions, especially in lower regularity class, is the core question in the area of fluid models. On the other hand, long-time behaviors of solutions including temporal decay and uniform-in-time damping stability are also of deep significance. These problems have been widely studied, however, the existing results are not regularity critical and the vanishing damping limit has not been discussed. The goal of this paper is to dig deeper in this direction and eventually establish the global existence of solutions in the critical low regularity space
u\in B^{1}_{\infty,1}
. Moreover, we aim to prove the uniform-in-time vanishing damping limit for
the inviscid Oldroyd-B model and discover the correlation between the sharp vanishing damping rate
and the temporal decay rate.

Assume that
d=2,3
and
a\in[0,1]
, we first establish local well-posedness for (
1.3
) in the sense of Hadamard with initial data

(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times B^{0}_{\infty,1}.

Then we divide the results of global theory into the following three cases.

Case 1: motivation and main ideas in proving Theorem
1.1
with damping
a>0
.

In this case, we consider the 2D inviscid
Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) with
a>0
. The motivation is to obtain global solutions for (
1.3
) with low regularity. We point out that damping effect is the key to reducing the regularity of
\tau
. We now establish the global existence of solutions of low regularity, where initial data belongs to
(L^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times(L^{2}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})
, which to our best knowledge, is novel in the literature.

By virtue of the Littlewood-Paley decomposition theory, we prove a new commutator estimate between the Riesz operator
\mathcal{R}_{i}
and the convection operator
u\cdot\nabla
:

(1.12)

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}).

Note that the commutator estimate of the Calderon-Zygmund operator is sharp, which does not require additional regularity about
\|\tau\|_{B^{\epsilon}_{\infty,1}}
with
{\epsilon}>0
like Lemma (
2.7
) does.

Since
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
is a critical space and
Q
does not have the transportation structure, product laws may require additional regularity. However, in the 2D case, we discover that
Q
can be controlled by a new estimation.
Using Bony’s decomposition, we have

(1.13)

\displaystyle\|Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}.

Combining (
1.12
) and (
1.13
), we close the global estimates with low regularity for (
1.3
) with
a>0
.

Next we introduce main difficulties and ideas in the process of proving global existence of the strong solutions with
a\in[0,1]
and uniform-in-time vanishing damping limit for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
).

Case 2: main difficulties and ideas in the 2D case with and without damping
a\in[0,1]
.

Difficulty (1):
Uniform-in-time control of some critical norms of the solutions.

In order to derive the dissipation of
u^{a}
,
a>0
is assumed in the proof of the global existence with the critical small condition. To obtain the global existence of the strong solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) with
a\in[0,1]
, we have to obtain the uniformly in time boundedness of some critical norms of the solution. Besides basic energy estimates, we used the inner product estimate to obtain the dissipation of velocity
u^{a}
.
Using the fact for
d=2
that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}(u^{a}\cdot\nabla)u^{a}\cdot\Delta u^{a}dx=0,

we close the global estimate with small data in
H^{1}
for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
), which implies the existence of the global weak solutions for
a\in[0,1]
.

To elevate weak solutions to strong solutions, the key lies in controlling the
L^{\infty}
norm of
\omega^{a}
, as indicated by the BKM principle
[
3
]
. By direct calculation, the vorticity satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\omega^{a}+u^{a}\cdot\nabla\omega^{a}=\nabla\times{\rm div}~\tau^{a}.

However, the term on the right-hand side,

\nabla\times{\rm div}~\tau^{a}
breaks the conservation laws and, more crucially, the equation does not capture the dissipative properties of the vorticity
\omega^{a}
. This makes it difficult to get global estimate of
\|\omega^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}
from the above equation directly. To overcome this difficulty, we use the following structural variable
\Gamma^{a}
introduced in
[
19
]
:

\Gamma^{a}=\omega^{a}-\mathcal{R}\tau^{a},

where
\mathcal{R}:=-(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm curl}~{\rm div}
. Note that once we aim to estimate the vorticity
\omega^{a}
through the structural variable
\Gamma^{a}
, the estimation of
\tau^{a}
becomes necessary.

By direct calculation,
\Gamma^{a}
satisfies the following transport equation with damping:

(1.14)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma^{a}+u^{a}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{a}=(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau^{a}+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})+[\mathcal{R},u^{a}\cdot\nabla]\tau^{a}.

The structural unknown
\Gamma^{a}
allows us to transfer dissipation
D(u^{a})
from the equation
\tau^{a}
to the equation of
\Gamma^{a}
, which helps to obtain a closed estimate of the global solution under smallness condition in the critical Besov space
B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. In addition, it also plays an important role in studying the solution of supercritical regularity and obtaining the key time integrability. When
a>0
, the existence of global strong solutions can be obtained by proving that
\Gamma
and
\tau
belong to
C([0,\infty);H^{s})
, as shown in
[
19
]
. However, the proof does not guarantee uniform boundedness of the
H^{s}
norm, posing an obstacle as
a\to 0
. Our strategy is to control
\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
directly.
Indeed, by Duhamel’s formula, we get

\tau^{a}(t)=e^{(\Delta-a)t}\tau_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(\Delta-a)(t-t^{\prime})}\left[Du^{a}-u^{a}\cdot\nabla\tau^{a}-Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})\right]dt^{\prime}.

Applying the Littlewood-Paley decomposition theory and utilizing the smoothing effect of the heat operator, we prove that
\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C.

Hence,
\|\omega^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
is uniformly bounded provided
\|\Gamma^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
is uniformly. By virtue of the estimates on (
1.14
), we conclude that

(1.15)

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1})}+\|(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq C(\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}).

Difficulty (2):
Extra regularity due to the convection term when considering vanishing damping limit.

When considering vanishing damping limit of the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) for any
T>0
, controlling the transport term
u^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
requires extra regularity. To overcome it, notice that global solution is uniformly bounded in time, see (
1.15
), we can elevate regularity of global solutions solely by improving regularity of initial data. Moreover, through precise estimates and the structural trick for the unknown
\Gamma^{a}
, we deduce that

(1.16)

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{2})}+\|(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\leq C(\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}).

The solution of supercritical regularity is also uniformly bounded in time and the bound depends linearly on the initial data, which is useful for considering vanishing damping limit for any
T>0
under the same topology.

With the results showing that the solutions are uniformly bounded in time, we first prove vanishing damping rate for low frequencies of the solutions. For any
T>0
, there holds

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}\leq C_{T}a.

In the general analysis of stability and the vanishing limit, a phenomenon of supercritical regularity arises when estimating the transport terms
u^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
. To handle this, we need to introduce a low-frequency truncated smoothing operator
S_{N}
to quantify the regularity, see
[
4
]
. Let
(u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})
be a solution of the inviscid Oldroyd-B model
(
1.3
)
with the initial data
(S_{N}u_{0},S_{N}\tau_{0})
and
a\in[0,1]
. Using (
1.15
) and (
1.16
) we deduce that for any
a\in[0,1]
and
T>0
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{T}\|(Id-S_{N})(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}},\quad\|\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0},\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{T}a2^{N}.

Combining the above inequalities, we conclude that

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1})}=0.

By virtue of the equation (
1.14
) of
\Gamma^{a}
and the time uniform bound of solutions with supercritical regularity, we also infer that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq C_{T}(\|(Id-S_{N})(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{(H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times(H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+a^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{N}).

This implies that

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Difficulty (3):
Establishing the time integrability of
\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
for initial data under critical regularity.

In this paper, we aim to explore and solve a newly observed problem: the uniform in time vanishing damping limit of the solution. Indeed, we first analyze the following quantity:

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2})}

as
a
approaches zero.
To tackle this problem, we find that the key to getting a uniform in time control is to obtain the time integrability of
\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
.

To do this, it is necessary to derive the decay rates for the low-frequency quantities, specifically
\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}
.
By virtue of the improved Fourier splitting method
[
16
]
, we obtain, for any
a\in[0,1]
,

(1.17)

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{2}}+(1+t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

However, we fail to derive the decay rates for
\|(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
directly due to the lack of regularity of initial data. A natural approach is to prove the time integrability of
\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
and
\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. Unfortunately, this approach fails for
d=2
, even under high regularity, as we can only have

\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{-1},

which is critically non-integrable. The main challenge lies in the low-frequency decay rate of
\tau^{a}
. To address this, we introduce a novel method of high-low frequency decomposition to get the following key integrability:

(1.18)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\leq C_{0}.

For the high-frequency component, we encounter no significant challenges. We have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\tau^{a}\|_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0},

With the help of the equation (
1.14
) of
\Gamma^{a}
again, we derive that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{0}^{t^{\prime}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t^{\prime}-s)}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dsdt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}.

Thus we get the integrability of the high-frequency part:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}.

Now it remains to control
\int_{1}^{\infty}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u^{a}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}dt.
Due to the smoothing effect of
\Delta_{-1}
, we can control it using energy norms. Applying time decay rates (
1.17
) together with the basic energy estimates, we first derive an integral form characterization of the decay effects:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{1-\delta}(\|\nabla u^{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau^{a}\|^{2}_{H^{1}})dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}\frac{1}{\delta},\quad\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{2-\delta}\|\nabla^{2}\tau^{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}\frac{1}{\delta},

where
\delta\in(0,1)
.
Furthermore, we can establish the time integrability of higher-order derivatives of the solutions to (
1.3
) as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{3}\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Note that there is a time growth on the right side of this inequality. For more details, one can see the proof of (
5.53
). We introduce a novel inequality to control this growth. By Lebesgue’s monotone convergence theorem, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u^{a}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}dt\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}\left\|\nabla u^{a}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u^{a}\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}dt\lesssim C_{0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{j}{8}}\lesssim C_{0}.

Consequently, we conclude that (
1.18
) holds true.

Difficulty (4):
Demonstrating the optimality of the uniform vanishing damping rate.

The discovery of the key integrability enables us to establish the uniform vanishing damping limit in
L^{2}
:

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}))}\lesssim a^{\frac{1}{2}},

Our goal is to show that the approximation rate
a^{\frac{1}{2}}
is indeed optimal. Heuristically speaking, one can consider the uniform vanishing damping limit for the damped heat equation:

\displaystyle\partial_{t}f^{a}-\Delta f^{a}+af^{a}=0,~~~~f^{a}|_{t=0}=f_{0},

where
a\geq 0
,
\hat{f_{0}}(\xi)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
with

\displaystyle\hat{f_{0}}(\xi)=\begin{cases}1,\quad|\xi|\leq 1,\\
0,\quad|\xi|\geq 2.\end{cases}

Then we infer that

\displaystyle\|f^{a}-f^{0}\|_{L^{2}}=(1-e^{-at})\|e^{\Delta t}f_{0}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim C_{0}(at)^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim C_{0}a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The reverse bound holds at
t=a^{-1}
. This scalar heat mode
is also an exact solution of the full nonlinear system:
take
u^{a}=0
,
\tau^{a}=f^{a}I_{d}
, and
P^{a}=f^{a}
.
The pressure balances
\operatorname{div}\tau^{a}=\nabla f^{a}
, and all
terms involving
\nabla u^{a}
vanish for every
b\in[-1,1]
.
Thus the heat scaling gives the sharp lower bound
a^{d/4}

in both dimensions, without a nonlinear perturbation argument;
see Propositions
5.9
and
6.5
.

Difficulty (5):
Obtaining the uniform vanishing damping limit in the same topology.

Furthermore, we also aim to obtain the uniform vanishing damping limit for the solutions in spaces of higher regularity. Specifically, we need to show that for any
0<\delta<1
, there exists a positive number
a_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim\delta,

whenever
a\leq a_{0}
.

By virtue of the mean-value theorem, along with the optimal decay rates and key
integrability, we deduce that for above
\delta>0
, there exists
T^{*}\in(0,\infty)
independent with
a\in[0,1]
, such that

(1.19)

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{(H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times(H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim\delta,\quad\text{for all}\ a\in[0,1],~t\geq T^{*}.

According to the result of vanishing damping limit in finite time interval, there exists
a_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T^{*};\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,T^{*};\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq\delta,

whenever
a\leq a_{0}
.
This together with (
1.19
) ensures that

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Case 3: main difficulties and ideas in the 3D case with and without damping
a\in[0,1]
.

For
d=3
, the convection term
u^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
becomes the main term, we encounter additional difficulty.

Difficulty (6):
Handling the transport term
u^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
in establishing the global estimates of solutions.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{a}\cdot\nabla)u^{a}\cdot\Delta u^{a}dx\neq 0,

we fail to close the global estimate for (
1.3
) in
H^{1}
with small data. By direct estimate, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u^{a}\cdot\nabla)u^{a}\cdot\Delta u^{a}dx\right|\lesssim\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla u^{a}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Hence we have to combine the estimate of
\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}
to close the energy estimates, which leads to the estimates of
\|\Gamma^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
and
\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
.
We have

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma^{a}+u^{a}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{a}+\frac{1}{2}\Gamma^{a}=(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau^{a}+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})+[\mathcal{R},u^{a}\cdot\nabla]\tau^{a}+\omega^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}.

For
d=3
, one can see that there is an external high-order nonlinear term
\omega^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
in the equation of
\Gamma^{a}
. Note that though
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
embeds into
L^{\infty}
, which satisfies the following algebraic property:

\|fg\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|f\|_{L^{\infty}}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}},

the critical Besov space
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
itself is not an algebra. However, by virtue of the divergence structure of
\omega^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
, we obtain the estimate:

\displaystyle\|\omega^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\|\omega^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}.

For any
a\in[0,1]
and
d=3
, fortunately, we close the global estimate for vorticity
\omega^{a}
through smallness condition in the critical Besov space
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
using this observation. This observation also plays a crucial role in considering vanishing damping limit in the same topology for any
T>0
.

Difficulty (7):
Obtaining optimal decay rates and time integrability of
\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
with critical regularity in the case
d=3
.

From now on, we investigate optimal decay rate of global solutions and prove the time integrability of
\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
for the inviscid Oldroyd-B equation (
1.3
) with critical regularity, which will be the key to obtain the uniform vanishing damping limit. For
d=3
, optimal time decay rates of global strong solutions with high regularity for the inviscid Oldroyd-B models were proved in
[
41
]
. However, due to low regularity of the solutions, the case
a\in[0,1]
is much more challenging. We will address the issues of consistent damping and low regularity by employing an improved Fourier splitting method alongside iteration techniques. We describe the iteration ideas in the following brief chart (The cubic term
\mathcal{N}(u,\tau)
is defined in (
6.11
)).

\displaystyle\text{Energy and Fourier splitting}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-1/8},\ (1+t)^{-1/4},\ (1+t)^{-3/8},

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-3/8}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sup_{t}\|(u,\tau)\|_{\dot{B}^{-3/2}_{2,\infty}}<\infty\longrightarrow\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-3/4},

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-3/4}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\text{key integrability}\longrightarrow\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|_{2}\lesssim(1+t)^{-5/4}.

Remark 1.11
.

Indeed, the following iteration techniques are essential in the mutual promotion between the decay rates and the low-frequency bounds in Fourier side. In the recent work of H. Jia, Z. Lei and the fourth author
[
28
]
, they have also discovered the mutual promotion between the decay rates and the spatial-weighted bounds on the physical side.

To conclude the proof in the 3D case, we utilize the established optimal decay rates and time integrability to obtain the uniform vanishing damping limit for
d=3
. Furthermore, we discover that the rate of the uniform vanishing damping limit in
L^{2}
is closely related to the time decay rate in
L^{2}
, similar to the observations made in the 2D case.

1.5.
Organization of the presenting paper

In Section 2, we list the notation and preliminaries which will be used in the sequel. In Section 3, we establish the local well-posedness in the sense of Hadamard with critical regularity. In Section 4, by virtue of the sharp commutator estimate for the Calderon-Zygmund operator, we establish global existence
for low regularity with damping.
In Section 5, we prove the global existence and uniform vanishing damping limit for the 2D
inviscid Oldroyd-B model. In Section 6, we study the global existence and uniform vanishing damping limit for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model with
d=3
. In Section 7, we provide some numerical results regarding the vanishing damping limit in periodic domains.

2.
Notations and preliminaries

In this section, we present our notations and introduce useful lemmas which will be used in the sequel.

2.1.
Notations

In this article, we adopt the following notation conventions:

(1)

We use
A\lesssim B
means
A\leq CB
for some absolute constant
C
. The notation
A\sim B
means
A\lesssim B
and
B\lesssim A
. Additionally,
A\lesssim_{\star}B
implies
A\leq CB
with
C
dependent on a specific quantity
\star
, i.e.,
C=C(\star)
.

(2)

For any two quantities
X
and
Y
, we shall denote
X\ll Y
if

X\leq cY
for some sufficiently small constant
c
. The smallness of the constant
c
is
usually clear from the context. The notation
X\gg Y
is similarly defined. Note that
our use of
\ll
and
\gg
here is
different
from the usual Vinogradov notation
in number theory or asymptotic analysis.

(3)

For two vectors
u,v
, we denote the inner product of
u,v
by
u\cdot v
. More precisely,

u\cdot v=\sum_{j}u_{j}v_{j}.

For two matrices
A,B
we denote the inner product of
A,B
by
A:B
, which is expressed by

A:B=\sum_{i,j}A_{ij}B_{ij}.

(4)

For a real-valued function
u:\Omega\to\mathbb{R}
we denote its usual Lebesgue
L^{p}
-norm by

\displaystyle\|u\|_{p}=\|u\|_{L^{p}(\Omega)}=\begin{cases}&\left(\int_{\Omega}|u|^{p}\ dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}},\quad 1\leq p<\infty;\\
&\operatorname{esssup}_{x\in\Omega}|u(x)|,\quad p=\infty.\end{cases}

(5)

Similarly, we use the weak derivative in the following sense: For
u
,
v\in L^{1}_{loc}(\Omega)
, (i.e they are locally integrable);
\forall\phi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\Omega)
, i.e
\phi
is infinitely differentiable (smooth) and compactly supported; and

\int_{\Omega}u(x)\ \partial^{\alpha}\phi(x)\ dx=(-1)^{\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{n}}\int_{\Omega}v(x)\ \phi(x)\ dx,

then
v
is defined to be the weak partial derivative of
u
, denoted by
\partial^{\alpha}u
.
Suppose
u\in L^{p}(\Omega)
and all weak derivatives
\partial^{\alpha}u
exist for
|\alpha|=\alpha_{1}+\cdots+\alpha_{n}\leq k
, such that
\partial^{\alpha}u\in L^{p}(\Omega)
for
|\alpha|\leq k
, then we denote
u\in W^{k,p}(\Omega)
to be the standard Sobolev space. The corresponding norm of
W^{k,p}(\Omega)
is :

\|u\|_{W^{k,p}(\Omega)}=\left(\sum_{|\alpha|\leq k}\int_{\Omega}|\partial^{\alpha}u|^{p}\ dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}\ .

For
p=2
case, we use the convention
H^{k}(\Omega)
to denote the space
W^{k,2}(\Omega)
. We often use
D^{m}u
to denote any differential operator
D^{\alpha}u
for any
|\alpha|=m
:
D^{2}
denotes
\partial_{x_{i}}\partial_{x_{j}}u
for
1\leq i,j\leq d
in particular.

(6)

In this paper we use the following convention for Fourier transform on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

f(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d}}\int_{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\hat{f}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}\ d\xi\ ,\ \widehat{f}(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x)e^{-i\xi\cdot x}\ dx\ .

Taking advantage of the Fourier expansion, we use the well-known equivalent
H^{s}
-norm and
\dot{H}^{s}
-semi-norm of function
f
by

\|f\|_{H^{s}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\|(1+|\xi|^{2})^{\frac{s}{2}}\hat{f}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}\ ,\ \|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}}\||\xi|^{s}\hat{f}(\xi)\|_{L^{2}}.

(7)

We sometimes adopt the notation
\Lambda=(-\Delta)^{\frac{1}{2}}
, which can be understood from the Fourier side:

\widehat{\Lambda f}(k)=|k|\widehat{f}(k).

Therefore, by Plancherel’s identity,
\|f\|_{\dot{H}^{s}}=\|\Lambda^{s}f\|_{L^{2}}
.

(8)

We use the
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
to denote the
L^{2}
inner product in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\langle f,g\rangle:=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}f(x)g(x)dx
.

2.2.
Preliminaries

We now recall the Littlewood-Paley decomposition theory and Besov spaces.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be the annulus
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}:\frac{3}{4}\leq|\xi|\leq\frac{8}{3}\}
. There exist radial functions
\chi
and
\varphi
, valued in the interval
[0,1]
, belonging respectively to
\mathscr{D}(B(0,\frac{4}{3}))
and
\mathscr{D}(\mathcal{C})
, and such that

\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ \chi(\xi)+\sum_{j\geq 0}\varphi(2^{-j}\xi)=1,

\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{0\},\ \sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi(2^{-j}\xi)=1,

|j-j^{\prime}|\geq 2\Rightarrow\mathrm{Supp}\ \varphi(2^{-j}\cdot)\cap\mathrm{Supp}\ \varphi(2^{-j^{\prime}}\cdot)=\emptyset,

j\geq 1\Rightarrow\mathrm{Supp}\ \chi(\cdot)\cap\mathrm{Supp}\ \varphi(2^{-j}\cdot)=\emptyset.

The set
\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}=B(0,\frac{2}{3})+\mathcal{C}
is an annulus, and we have

|j-j^{\prime}|\geq 5\Rightarrow 2^{j}\mathcal{C}\cap 2^{j^{\prime}}\widetilde{\mathcal{C}}=\emptyset.

Further, we have

\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\ \frac{1}{2}\leq\chi^{2}(\xi)+\sum_{j\geq 0}\varphi^{2}(2^{-j}\xi)\leq 1,

\forall\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\backslash\{0\},\ \frac{1}{2}\leq\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varphi^{2}(2^{-j}\xi)\leq 1.

\mathscr{F}
represents the Fourier transform and its inverse is denoted by
\mathscr{F}^{-1}
.
Let
u
be a tempered distribution in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. For all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, define

\Delta_{j}u=0\,\ \text{if}\,\ j\leq-2,~~\Delta_{-1}u=\mathscr{F}^{-1}(\chi\mathscr{F}u),~~\Delta_{j}u=\mathscr{F}^{-1}(\varphi(2^{-j}\cdot)\mathscr{F}u)\,\ \text{if}\,\ j\geq 0,~~S_{j}u=\sum_{j^{\prime}<j}\Delta_{j^{\prime}}u.

Then the Littlewood-Paley decomposition is given as follows:

u=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\Delta_{j}u\quad\text{in}\ \mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Let
s\in\mathbb{R},\ 1\leq p,r\leq\infty.
The non-homogeneous Besov spaces
B^{s}_{p,r}
and
B^{s}_{p,r}(\mathcal{L}^{p^{\prime}})
are defined by

B^{s}_{p,r}=\{u\in S^{\prime}:\|u\|_{B^{s}_{p,r}}=\Big\|(2^{js}\|\Delta_{j}u\|_{L^{p}})_{j}\Big\|_{l^{r}(\mathbb{Z})}<\infty\}.

For any positive time
T
, the Time-Space Besov Spaces are defined by

\displaystyle L^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})=\{u\in S^{\prime}:\|u\|_{L^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})}=\left\|\left\|2^{js}\|\Delta_{j}u\|_{L^{p}}\right\|_{l^{r}({\mathbb{Z}})}\right\|_{L^{\rho}_{T}}<\infty\},

and

\displaystyle\tilde{L}^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})=\{u\in S^{\prime}:\|u\|_{\tilde{L}^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})}=\left\|2^{js}\|\Delta_{j}u\|_{L^{\rho}_{T}(L^{p})}\right\|_{l^{r}({\mathbb{Z}})}<\infty\}.

Moreover, the following embedding relationships hold:

L^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})\hookrightarrow\tilde{L}^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})~~~~\text{if}~~~~r\geq\rho~~~~\text{and}~~~~\tilde{L}^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})\hookrightarrow L^{\rho}_{T}(B^{s}_{p,r})~~~~\text{if}~~~~r\leq\rho.

Let
u
and
v
be tempered distributions in
\mathscr{S}^{\prime}
, then the non-homogeneous
\rm paraproduct
of
v
by
u
is defined as follows:

\displaystyle T_{u}v=\sum_{j}S_{j-1}u\Delta_{j}v,

and the non-homogeneous
\rm remainder
of
u
and
v
is defined by

\displaystyle R(u,v)=\sum_{|k-j|\leq 1}\Delta_{k}u\Delta_{j}v\triangleq\sum_{k\geq-1}\Delta_{k}u\tilde{\Delta}_{k}v.

At least formally, we obtain the so-called Bony’s decomposition:

\displaystyle uv=T_{u}v+T_{v}u+R(u,v).

Lemma 2.2
.

For any
(s,t)\in\mathbb{R}\times(-\infty,0)
and
(p,p_{1},p_{2},r,r_{1},r_{2})\in[1,\infty]^{6}
, there exists a constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\|T_{u}v\|_{B^{s}_{p,r}}\leq C^{1+|s|}\|u\|_{L^{p_{1}}}\|v\|_{B^{s}_{p_{2},r}},

with
(u,v)\in L^{p_{1}}\times B^{s}_{p_{2},r}
and
\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\|T_{u}v\|_{B^{s+t}_{p,r}}\leq\frac{C^{1+|s+t|}}{-t}\|u\|_{B^{t}_{\infty,r_{1}}}\|v\|_{B^{s}_{p,r_{2}}},

with
(u,v)\in B^{t}_{\infty,r_{1}}\times B^{s}_{p,r_{2}}
and
\frac{1}{r}=\min\{1,\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}\}
.

Lemma 2.3
.

A constant
C
exists which satisfies the following inequalities. Let
(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
(p_{1},p_{2},r_{1},r_{2})\in[1,\infty]^{4}
. Assume that

\frac{1}{p}=\frac{1}{p_{1}}+\frac{1}{p_{2}}\leq 1~~~~~\text{and}~~~~~\frac{1}{r}=\frac{1}{r_{1}}+\frac{1}{r_{2}}\leq 1.

If
s_{1}+s_{2}>0
, for any
(u,v)\in B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},r_{1}}\times B^{s_{2}}_{p_{2},r_{2}}
, then we have

\displaystyle\|R(u,v)\|_{B^{s_{1}+s_{2}}_{p,r}}\leq\frac{C^{1+|s_{1}+s_{2}|}}{s_{1}+s_{2}}\|u\|_{B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},r_{1}}}\|v\|_{B^{s_{2}}_{p_{2},r_{2}}}.

If
r=1
and
s_{1}+s_{2}=0
, for any
(u,v)\in B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},r_{1}}\times B^{s_{2}}_{p_{2},r_{2}}
, then we have

\displaystyle\|R(u,v)\|_{B^{0}_{p,\infty}}\leq C\|u\|_{B^{s_{1}}_{p_{1},r_{1}}}\|v\|_{B^{s_{2}}_{p_{2},r_{2}}}.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
1\leq p\leq\infty,\ 1\leq r\leq\infty,\ \sigma>-1-d\min(\frac{1}{p},\frac{1}{p^{\prime}})
and
\rm div~u=0
. Define
I_{j}=[u\cdot\nabla,\Delta_{j}]f
. There exists a constant
C
such that for
\sigma>0
, we have

\Big\|(2^{j\sigma}\|I_{j}\|_{L^{p}_{x}})_{j}\Big\|_{l^{r}(\mathbb{Z})}\leq C(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|f\|_{B^{\sigma}_{p,r}}+\|\nabla u\|_{B^{\sigma-1}_{p,r}}\|\nabla_{x}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}).

If
\sigma>1+\frac{d}{p}
or
\sigma=1+\frac{d}{p},~r=1
, we have

\Big\|(2^{j\sigma}\|I_{j}\|_{L^{p}_{x}})_{j}\Big\|_{l^{r}(\mathbb{Z})}\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{B^{\sigma-1}_{p,r}}\|f\|_{B^{\sigma}_{p,r}}.

If
\sigma<1+\frac{d}{p}
, we have

\Big\|(2^{j\sigma}\|I_{j}\|_{L^{p}_{x}})_{j}\Big\|_{l^{r}(\mathbb{Z})}\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{B^{\frac{d}{p}}_{p,r}\cap L^{\infty}}\|f\|_{B^{\sigma}_{p,r}}.

We have the following product rules.

Lemma 2.5
.

For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
C>0
such that

\|uv\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|v\|_{B^{\epsilon}_{\infty,\infty}}).

Let
{\rm div}~u=0
. For nonlinear terms that satisfy the transportation structure, the accurate estimate hold

\|u\cdot\nabla v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\|u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|v\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}.

We introduce the following lemma to describe the action of the heat kernel.

Lemma 2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an annulus. Positive constants
c
and
C
exist such that for any
p\in[1,+\infty]
and any couple
(t,\lambda)
of positive real numbers, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Supp}~\hat{u}\subset\lambda\mathcal{C}\Rightarrow\|e^{t\Delta}u\|_{L^{p}}\leq Ce^{-ct\lambda^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{p}}.

The following commutator lemma is useful to estimate the structural unknown
\Gamma
.

Lemma 2.7
.

Let
{\rm div}~u=0
and
\mathcal{R}=-(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm curl}~{\rm div}
. For every
(r,p)\in[1,\infty]\times(1,\infty)
and
\epsilon>0
, there exists a constant
C=C(r,p,\epsilon)
such that

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,r}}\leq C(\|\omega\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\omega\|_{L^{p}})(\|\tau\|_{B^{\epsilon}_{\infty,r}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{p}}).

The following facts can be proved by the standard properties of Calderon-Zygmund operator.

Lemma 2.8
.

(1) Let
\operatorname{div}v=0
and
\omega=\operatorname{curl}v
. For any
a\in[1,\infty)
and
b\in[1,\infty]
, there exists positive constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla v\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\min\{\|\omega\|_{L^{a}},\|v\|_{L^{b}}\}.

(2) For all
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
1\leq p,r\leq\infty
, there exists a constant
C
such that

\displaystyle\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}f\|_{B^{s}_{p,r}}\leq C\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,r}}.

We also present following estimates for the transport equations, which will be frequently used in the subsequent chapters.

Lemma 2.9
.

Suppose
f
satisfies the following transport equation:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}f+v\cdot\nabla f=g\\
f|_{t=0}=f_{0}\end{array}\right.

with
v
divergence free. Then the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\|f\|_{B^{\sigma}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\|f_{0}\|_{B^{\sigma}_{\infty,1}}e^{C\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds}+C\int_{0}^{t}e^{C\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}}\|g\|_{B^{\sigma}_{\infty,1}}ds,

where
\sigma\in[0,1]
.

The proof to the lemmas above are standard and we refer the readers to Bahouri-Chemin-Danchin
[
2
]
, Guo-Li-Yin
[
23
]
, Hmidi-Keraani-Rousset
[
26
]
, Li
[
32
]
and Li-Sire
[
33
]
for the details.

3.
Local well-posedness with critical regularity

In this section we show the local well-posedness of (
1
) in both 2D and 3D cases. In fact as will be clear, our proof does not depend on the dimension. To start with we introduce the following lemma which will be useful in dealing with the pressure term
\nabla P
.

Lemma 3.1
.

[
2
]

For any
-1<\sigma\leq 1
, there exists a constant
C
such that if
\rm div~u=\rm div~v=0
, then

\displaystyle\|\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\rm div(u\cdot\nabla v)\|_{B^{\sigma}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\min(\|u\|_{B^{\sigma}_{\infty,1}}\|v\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}},\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|v\|_{B^{\sigma}_{\infty,1}}).

We are interested in the well-posedness of
(
1.3
)
in the sense of Hadamard. Since the local estimates are uniformly in
a
, we may drop the upper index
a
of the solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) without any confusions.
For the entire-space problem in the inhomogeneous Besov classes below,
we fix the pressure gradient by

(3.1)

\nabla P=\nabla\Delta^{-1}\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}(\tau-u\otimes u).

The multiplier is defined by the usual low–high frequency localization
and annihilates spatial constants; no additional harmonic pressure
gradient is allowed. All uniqueness statements in these classes refer
to this normalization. The low-frequency part is well defined on bounded
inputs by the integrable kernel used in (
3.8
) below.

Definition 3.2

(Well-posedness)
.

The Cauchy problem (
1.3
) is said to be locally well-posed
in a metric space
\mathbb{X}
if for any
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in\mathbb{X}
, there exists a neighbourhood
U
of
(u_{0},\tau_{0})

and
T=T(U)>0
such that for any
(v_{0},\sigma_{0})\in U
, we have

(i) (Existence): There exists a distributional solution
(u,\tau)
in
E
for some
E\subset C([0,T):\mathbb{X})
to (
1.3
).

(ii) (Uniqueness): The solution
(u,\tau)
is unique in
E
.

(iii) (Continuous dependence): The solution map
(v_{0},\sigma_{0})\rightarrow(v,\sigma)
is continuous from

U
to
C([0,T):\mathbb{X})
.

We now establish local well-posedness for (
1.3
) in the sense of Hadamard with critical regularity.

Proposition 3.3
.

Let
d\geq 2
and
a\in[0,1]
. For any divergence-free
u_{0}\in B^{1}_{\infty,1}
and symmetric
\tau_{0}\in B^{0}_{\infty,1}
, there exists a time
T>0
, which is independent of damping coefficient
a
, such that
(\ref{eq0})
has a unique solution
(u,\tau)\in C([0,T);B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times\bigl(C([0,T);B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}([0,T);B^{2}_{\infty,1})\bigr)
. Moreover, the
solution depends continuously on the initial data.

Remark 3.4
.

For the transport equation, the velocity
u\in C^{0,1}
is the key to persisting regularity. Notice that
B^{1}_{\infty,1}\hookrightarrow C^{0,1}
. Under the conditions in Proposition
3.3
, if additionally
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in H^{k}
with
k\geq 0
, one can deduce that the solution
(u,\tau)\in C([0,T);H^{k})
. This means that we establish local well-posedness for (
1.3
) in
(B^{1}_{\infty,1}\cap H^{k})\times(B^{0}_{\infty,1}\cap H^{k})
.

Proof.

Let
(u^{0},\tau^{0})=(S_{0}u_{0},S_{0}\tau_{0})
. We define a sequence
(u^{n},\tau^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
by solving the following
linear equations:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u^{n+1}+u^{n}\cdot\nabla u^{n+1}+\nabla P^{n+1}={\rm div}~\tau^{n+1},~~~~{\rm div}~u^{n+1}=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}\tau^{n+1}+u^{n}\cdot\nabla\tau^{n+1}+a\tau^{n+1}+Q(\nabla u^{n},\tau^{n+1})=D(u^{n})+\Delta\tau^{n+1},\\[4.30554pt]
u^{n+1}|_{t=0}=S_{n+1}u_{0},~~\tau^{n+1}|_{t=0}=S_{n+1}\tau_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Step 1: Uniform bounds.

Applying
\Delta_{j}
to
\eqref{3eq1}_{1}
, for any
j\geq-1
, we have

(3.5)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Delta_{j}u^{n+1}+u^{n}\cdot\nabla\Delta_{j}u^{n+1}=-[\Delta_{j},u^{n}\cdot\nabla]u^{n+1}+\Delta_{j}{\rm div}~\tau^{n+1}-\Delta_{j}\nabla P^{n+1}.

Integrating
(\ref{3eq2})
on
[0,T]
, we infer that

(3.6)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}u^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{j}u_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|[\Delta_{j},u^{n}\cdot\nabla]u^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\|\Delta_{j}{\rm div}\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Delta_{j}\nabla P^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt.

Multiplying both sides of (
3.6
) by
2^{j}
and summing up
j
, we obtain

(3.7)

\displaystyle\|u^{n+1}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j\geq-1}2^{j}\|[\Delta_{j},u^{n}\cdot\nabla]u^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla P^{n+1}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt.

By virtue of Lemma
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq-1}2^{j}\|[\Delta_{j},u^{n}\cdot\nabla]u^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|u^{n}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|u^{n+1}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}.

Since
{\rm div}~u^{n}={\rm div}~u^{n+1}=0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\nabla P^{n+1}=\nabla\Delta^{-1}{\rm div}~{\rm div}~(\tau^{n+1}-u^{n}\otimes u^{n+1}).

By virtue of Lemma
3.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\nabla\Delta^{-1}{\rm div}~{\rm div}~(u^{n}\otimes u^{n+1})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\|u^{n}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|u^{n+1}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}.

By Lemma
2.8
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm div}~{\rm div}~\tau^{n+1}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}},

and

(3.8)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm div}~{\rm div}~\tau^{n+1}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm div}~{\rm div}~\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\theta E_{d}\ast\Delta_{-1}\nabla{\rm div}~{\rm div}~\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle~~~+\|\nabla{\rm div}~{\rm div}~(1-\theta)E_{d}\ast\Delta_{-1}\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{-1}\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}.

Hence, we deduce from (
3.7
)-(
3.8
) that

(3.9)

\displaystyle\|u^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\leq\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+CT\|u^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\|u^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}dt.

Applying
\Delta_{j}
to
\eqref{3eq1}_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}\tau^{n+1}=e^{\Delta t-at}\Delta_{j}\tau^{n+1}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{\Delta(t-s)-a(t-s)}\Delta_{j}(-Q(\nabla u^{n},\tau^{n+1})-u^{n}\cdot\nabla\tau^{n+1}+D(u^{n}))ds.

By virtue of Lemmas
2.6
and
2.5
, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we infer that

\displaystyle\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{2j}\|\Delta_{j}\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{T}2^{-2}\|\Delta_{-1}\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\leq(1+T)\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{2j}\|\Delta_{j}\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C(1+T)\big(\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|Q(\nabla u^{n},\tau^{n+1})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\|u^{n}\cdot\nabla\tau^{n+1}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla u^{n}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt\big)

\displaystyle\leq C(1+T)\big(\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\|u^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}(T+T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})})\big).

Note that

\displaystyle\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{2}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim\|\tau^{n+1}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\|\tau^{n+1}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}.

Assuming that

\displaystyle\|u^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}\leq 4C(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}),

and

(3.10)

\displaystyle T=\min\left\{\frac{1}{100C^{2}},\frac{1}{100C^{4}(1+\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})^{4}}\right\},

where
C>1
. Then we conclude from (
3.9
)-(
3.10
) that

\displaystyle\|u^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau^{n+1}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}\leq 3C(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}).

We thus finish the proof of the uniform bounds for sequence
(u^{n},\tau^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Step 2: Convergence.

Denote
\overline{f}^{n}\triangleq f^{n+1}-f^{n}
. For
n\geq 1
, subtracting two consecutive systems in (
3
) gives

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}\overline{u}^{n}+u^{n}\cdot\nabla\overline{u}^{n}+\nabla\overline{P}^{n}={\rm div}~\overline{\tau}^{n}-\overline{u}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla u^{n},~~~~{\rm div}~\overline{u}^{n}=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}\overline{\tau}^{n}+u^{n}\cdot\nabla\overline{\tau}^{n}+a\overline{\tau}^{n}+Q(\nabla u^{n},\overline{\tau}^{n})+Q(\nabla\overline{u}^{n-1},\tau^{n})=D(\overline{u}^{n-1})+\Delta\overline{\tau}^{n}-\overline{u}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\tau^{n},\\[4.30554pt]
\overline{u}^{n}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{n}u_{0},~~\overline{\tau}^{n}|_{t=0}=\Delta_{n}\tau_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Using Lemmas
2.4
and
3.1
, we obtain

(3.11)

\displaystyle\|\overline{u}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Delta_{n}u_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+CT\|u^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}(\|\overline{u}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\overline{u}^{n-1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})})

\displaystyle~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt.

By virtue of Lemmas
2.6
and
2.5
, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we infer that

(3.12)

\displaystyle\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{T}2^{-1}\|\Delta_{-1}\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\leq(1+T)\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\Delta_{n}\tau_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|Q(\nabla u^{n},\overline{\tau}^{n})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q(\nabla\overline{u}^{n-1},\tau^{n})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\|u^{n}\cdot\nabla\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\overline{u}^{n-1}\cdot\nabla\tau^{n}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\overline{u}^{n-1}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Delta_{n}\tau_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+C\|\overline{u}^{n-1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}(T+\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty,1})})

\displaystyle~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\|Q(\nabla u^{n},\overline{\tau}^{n})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q(\nabla\overline{u}^{n-1},\tau^{n})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}dt

\displaystyle~~~+C\|u^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Delta_{n}\tau_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+C\|\overline{u}^{n-1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}(T+\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty,1})})

\displaystyle~~~+C\|u^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty,1})}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|Q(\nabla\overline{u}^{n-1},\tau^{n})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}dt.

Using Lemma
2.5
, we have

(3.13)

\displaystyle\|Q(\nabla\overline{u}^{n-1},\tau^{n})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\partial_{k}Q(\overline{u}^{n-1},\tau^{n})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q(\overline{u}^{n-1},\partial_{k}\tau^{n})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|Q(\overline{u}^{n-1},\tau^{n})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q(\overline{u}^{n-1},\nabla\tau^{n})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\overline{u}^{n-1}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau^{n}\|_{B^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\infty,1}}.

Set

M=\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}},\qquad U_{n}=\|\overline{u}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})},

Z_{n}=\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\overline{\tau}^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})},\qquad\rho(T)=C\bigl(T+MT^{1/4}\bigr).

Interpolation and Step 1 imply

\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{3/2}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim T^{1/4}\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}^{1/4}\|\tau^{n}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}^{3/4}\lesssim MT^{1/4}.

Combining (
3.12
)–(
3.13
) and absorbing the terms
containing
Z_{n}
, we obtain, for
n\geq 1
,

(3.14)

\displaystyle Z_{n}\leq CM2^{-n}+\rho(T)U_{n-1}.

The term
CT
in
\rho(T)
accounts for the linear source

D(\overline{u}^{n-1})
. Together with (
3.11
), this gives, after
increasing
C
in the definition of
\rho(T)
,

U_{n}\leq CM2^{-n}+\rho(T)U_{n-1}.

Choose the constant in (
3.10
) sufficiently large so that

\rho(T)\leq 1/2
. The zeroth difference satisfies
U_{0}+Z_{0}\leq CM

directly by Step 1. Consequently,

\sum_{n=0}^{m}U_{n}\leq CM+\rho(T)\sum_{n=0}^{m-1}U_{n},\qquad\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(U_{n}+Z_{n})\leq CM.

Hence, we deduce that
(u^{n},\tau^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{\infty}([0,T);B^{0}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}([0,T);B^{-1}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}([0,T);B^{1}_{\infty,1})
.
Uniform bounds for
(u^{n},\tau^{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in Besov spaces ensure that there exists
(u,\tau)\in L^{\infty}([0,T);B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}([0,T);B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}([0,T);B^{2}_{\infty,1})
satisfying (
1.3
) such that

\displaystyle u^{n}\rightarrow u~~~~\text{in}~~~~L^{\infty}([0,T);B^{s^{\prime}}_{\infty,1})~~~~\text{and}~~~~\tau^{n}\rightarrow\tau~~~~\text{in}~~~~L^{\infty}([0,T);B^{s^{\prime}-1}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}([0,T);B^{s^{\prime}+1}_{\infty,1}),

where
s^{\prime}<1
. Note that the solutions
(u,\tau)
also belong to
C([0,T);B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times C([0,T);B^{0}_{\infty,1})
. Moreover, if
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in B^{s}_{\infty,1}\times B^{s-1}_{\infty,1}
for any
s>1
, similar to
step 1
,
then there exists
C>0
, depending on
s,d
and the bounded critical norms but not on the higher initial norms, such that

(3.15)

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{s}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{s-1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{s+1}_{\infty,1})}\leq C(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{s}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{s-1}_{\infty,1}}).

Step 3: Uniqueness.

Let
(u,\tau)
and
(v,\sigma)
be two solutions of
(
1.3
)
with the initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
and
(v_{0},\sigma_{0})
. Then we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}(u-v)+u\cdot\nabla(u-v)+\nabla(P_{u}-P_{v})={\rm div}~(\tau-\sigma)-(u-v)\cdot\nabla v,~~~~{\rm div}~(u-v)=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}(\tau-\sigma)+u\cdot\nabla(\tau-\sigma)-\Delta(\tau-\sigma)+a(\tau-\sigma)+Q(\nabla u,\tau-\sigma)\\[4.30554pt]
=D(u-v)-(u-v)\cdot\nabla\sigma-Q(\nabla(u-v),\sigma),\\[4.30554pt]
(u-v)|_{t=0}=u_{0}-v_{0},~~(\tau-\sigma)|_{t=0}=\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Using Lemmas
2.4
and
3.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|u-v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}(\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|v\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}})+\|\tau-\sigma\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt

\displaystyle\leq\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\frac{1}{2}\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+C\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})},

which implies that

(3.20)

\displaystyle\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq C\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}.

By virtue of Lemmas
2.6
and
2.5
, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we infer from (
3.13
) that

(3.21)

\displaystyle\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq(1+T)\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\int_{0}^{T}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}(\tau-\sigma)\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|Q(\nabla u,\tau-\sigma)\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\|Q(\nabla(u-v),\sigma)\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|u\cdot\nabla(\tau-\sigma)\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\|(u-v)\cdot\nabla\sigma\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|u-v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|Q(\nabla(u-v),\sigma)\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}dt

\displaystyle~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\|u-v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}(1+\|\sigma\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}})+\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau-\sigma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|u-v\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}(1+\|\sigma\|_{B^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\infty,1}})dt

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{T}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau-\sigma\|_{B^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty,1}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\frac{1}{4C}\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{4}(\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}).

Combining (
3.20
) and (
3.21
), we conclude that

(3.22)

\displaystyle\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\leq C\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}.

We thus prove the uniqueness for (
1.3
) on
[0,T]
.

Step 4: Continuous dependence.

Assume that
(u,\tau)
and
(v,\sigma)
are two solutions of
(
1.3
)
with the initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
and
(v_{0},\sigma_{0})
. Due to the difficulty of exceeding regularity, we introduce a smoothing operator
S_{N}
to quantify regularity, see
[
4
]
. Let
(u_{N},\tau_{N})
and
(v_{N},\sigma_{N})
be two solutions of
(
1.3
)
with the initial data
(S_{N}u_{0},S_{N}\tau_{0})
and
(S_{N}v_{0},S_{N}\sigma_{0})
. Similar to
Step 3
, we deduce from (
3.22
) that

(3.23)

\displaystyle\|u-u_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau-\tau_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C2^{-N}(\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}).

and

\displaystyle\|v-v_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\sigma-\sigma_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(Id-S_{N})v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\|(Id-S_{N})\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C2^{-N}(\|(Id-S_{N})v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}).

Replacing
(v,\sigma)
with
(u_{N},\tau_{N})
in (
3
), by Lemmas
2.4
and
3.1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|(u-u_{N})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|u-u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}(\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}})dt

\displaystyle~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\|(u-u_{N})\cdot\nabla u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau-\tau_{N}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}dt.

According to Lemmas
2.2
and
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|(u-u_{N})\cdot\nabla u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u-u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|u-u_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}.

According to (
3.23
), (
3.15
), we infer that

\displaystyle\|u-u_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}).

The above inequalities ensure that

(3.24)

\displaystyle\|u-u_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\leq C(\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})+C\|\tau-\tau_{N}\|_{L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}.

By virtue of Lemmas
2.6
and
2.5
, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we infer that

(3.25)

\displaystyle\|\tau-\tau_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq C\bigg(\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{T}\|Q(\nabla u,\tau-\tau_{N})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\|Q(\nabla(u-u_{N}),\tau_{N})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u\cdot\nabla(\tau-\tau_{N})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\|(u-u_{N})\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u-u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt\bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\int_{0}^{T}\|u-u_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}(1+\|\tau_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}})dt

\displaystyle~~~+C\int_{0}^{T}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau-\tau_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt

\displaystyle\leq C\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\frac{1}{4C}\|u-u_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle~~~+\frac{1}{4}\|\tau-\tau_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}.

According to (
3.24
) and (
3.25
), we obtain

(3.26)

\displaystyle\|u-u_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau-\tau_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}).

Analogously, we have

\displaystyle\|v-v_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\sigma-\sigma_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|(Id-S_{N})v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}).

According to the interpolation inequality, (
3.15
) and (
3.22
), we know

(3.27)

\displaystyle\|u_{N}-v_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau_{N}-\sigma_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|u_{N}-v_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau_{N}-\sigma_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{-1}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|u_{N}-v_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle+\|\tau_{N}-\sigma_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{3}_{\infty,1})})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C2^{\frac{N}{2}}(\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining (
3.26
)-(
3.27
), we conclude that

(3.28)

\displaystyle\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})

\displaystyle+C2^{\frac{N}{2}}(\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})^{\frac{1}{2}}.

For any
R>0
, we consider
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in B_{R}=\{(f,g)\in B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times B^{0}_{\infty,1}:\|(f,g)\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq R\}
. Then we will prove that the solution map
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
to
(u,\tau)
is continuous from
B_{R}
to
C([0,T];B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times B^{0}_{\infty,1})
uniformly with respect to
a
. Note that
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\|(Id-S_{N})f\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}=0
. For any
{\epsilon}>0
, there exists a
M({\epsilon},(u_{0},\tau_{0}),R)>0
, for any
N\geq M
, there holds

C(\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})\leq\frac{{\epsilon}}{4}.

Then, there exists a
\delta({\epsilon},N,(u_{0},\tau_{0}),R)>0
such that for any
\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq\delta
, there holds

\displaystyle C(\|(Id-S_{N})v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})

\displaystyle\leq C(\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})

\displaystyle~~~+C(\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\epsilon}}{4}+C\delta\leq\frac{{\epsilon}}{2},

and

C2^{\frac{N}{2}}\left(\|u_{0}-v_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}-\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\|u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|v_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\sigma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{{\epsilon}}{4}.

One can infer from (
3.28
) that

\|u-v\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau-\sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{T}(B^{2}_{\infty,1})}\leq{\epsilon}.

This completes the proof of Proposition
3.3
.
∎

4.
Global existence of solutions of low regularity in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with damping

In this section, we consider the 2D inviscid
Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) with
a>0
and prove Theorem
1.1
. We point out that damping effect is the key to reducing the regularity of
\tau
; indeed we then proved the global well-posedness of lowered regularity with help of our new observation.

Denote
A(D)f=\mathcal{F}^{-1}(A(\xi)\widehat{f})
. In the next proposition, we recall some properties of the Riesz operator.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{i}
be the Riesz operator
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\frac{\partial_{i}}{|D|}
. Then the following hold true.

(1) For any
p\in(1,\infty)
, there exists a positive constant
C(p)
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}(L^{p})}\leq C.

(2) Let
\chi\in\mathcal{D}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be radial and fix
s>0
.
There exists
C=C(d,s,\chi)
such that

\displaystyle\||D|^{s}\chi(2^{-q}|D|)\mathcal{R}_{i}\|_{\mathcal{L}(L^{p})}\leq C2^{qs},

for every
p\in[1,\infty]
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
.

(3) Let
\mathcal{C}
be a fixed ring. Then, there exists
g\in\mathcal{S}
whose spectrum does not meet the origin such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{i}f=2^{qd}g(2^{q}\dot{)}\ast f,

for any
f
with Fourier transform supported in
2^{q}\mathcal{C}
.

As explained in the introduction, we give the proof of a new commutator lemma between the Riesz operator
\mathcal{R}_{i}
and the convection operator
u\cdot\nabla
, which plays a crucial role in reducing the regularity. Note that the following commutator lemma of Calderon-Zygmund operator is sharp, which does not require additional regularity of
\|\tau\|_{B^{\epsilon}_{\infty,1}}
with
{\epsilon}>0
as in Lemma
2.7
. For more details, one can refer to
[
26
,
32
,
33
]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
\mathcal{R}_{i}
be the Riesz operator
\mathcal{R}_{i}=\frac{\partial_{i}}{|D|}
and assume
\operatorname{div}u=0
,
u,\tau\in L^{2}
,
\nabla u\in L^{\infty}
, and
\tau\in B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R}_{i},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}).

Proof.

Firstly, by Bony’s decomposition, we split the commutator into three parts:

\displaystyle[\mathcal{R}_{i},u\cdot\nabla]\tau

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 0}[\mathcal{R}_{i},S_{j-1}u\cdot\nabla]\Delta_{j}\tau+\sum_{j\geq 0}[\mathcal{R}_{i},\Delta_{j}u\cdot\nabla]S_{j-1}\tau+\sum_{j\geq-1}[\mathcal{R}_{i},\Delta_{j}u\cdot\nabla]\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{j\geq 0}A^{1}_{j}+\sum_{j\geq 0}A^{2}_{j}+\sum_{j\geq-1}A^{3}_{j},

where
\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau=\sum_{k=j-1}^{j+1}\Delta_{k}\tau
.
By Lemma
4.1
, we know that there exists
g\in\mathcal{S}
whose spectrum does not meet the origin such that

\displaystyle A^{1}_{j}=g_{j}\ast\left(S_{j-1}u\cdot\nabla\Delta_{j}\tau\right)-S_{j-1}u\cdot\left(g_{j}\ast\nabla\Delta_{j}\tau\right),

where
g_{j}(x)=2^{dj}g(2^{j}x)
. According to Bernstein’s inequality and
\|xg_{j}\|_{L^{1}}=2^{-j}\|xg\|_{L^{1}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|A^{1}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\|g_{j}\ast\left(S_{j-1}u\cdot\nabla\Delta_{j}\tau\right)-S_{j-1}u\cdot\left(g_{j}\ast\nabla\Delta_{j}\tau\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{j}(x-y)(S_{j-1}u(y)-S_{j-1}u(x))\cdot\nabla\Delta_{j}\tau(y)dy\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle=\left\|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}g_{j}(x-y)[(y-x)\cdot\nabla S_{j-1}u(x+t(y-x))]\cdot\nabla\Delta_{j}\tau(y)dy\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|xg_{j}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla S_{j-1}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\Delta_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\Delta_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}.

Then we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq-1}\|\Delta_{k}\sum_{j\geq 0}A^{1}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\sum_{j\geq 0}\|A^{1}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

For the second paraproduct, retain the Riesz multiplier on the
low-frequency factor:

\displaystyle A_{j}^{2}={}

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{i}(\Delta_{j}u\cdot\nabla S_{j-1}\tau)-\Delta_{j}u\cdot\mathcal{R}_{i}\nabla S_{j-1}\tau.

The first product has Fourier support in an annulus of size
2^{j}
,
whereas
S_{j-1}\tau
need not be supported away from the origin.
We therefore estimate the two terms separately. Annular multiplier
bounds and a dyadic decomposition of the low-frequency factor give

\displaystyle\|A_{j}^{2}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{j}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{-1\leq q\leq j-2}2^{q}\|\Delta_{q}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{-1\leq q\leq j-2}2^{q}\|\Delta_{q}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}.

For
q=-1
, the multiplier
\mathcal{R}_{i}\nabla\Delta_{-1}

has an integrable convolution kernel, so the same bound applies.
Both terms in
A_{j}^{2}
have annular output support. Consequently,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq-1}\left\|\Delta_{k}\sum_{j\geq 0}A_{j}^{2}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{j\geq 0}\sum_{-1\leq q\leq j-2}2^{q-j}\|\Delta_{q}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

Using
{\rm div}~u=0
, we rewrite
A^{3}_{j}
as

(4.1)

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geq-1}A^{3}_{j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 2}\mathcal{R}_{i}{\rm div}~(\Delta_{j}u\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau)-\sum_{j\geq 2}{\rm div}~(\Delta_{j}u\mathcal{R}_{i}\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau)+\sum_{j\leq 1}[\mathcal{R}_{i},\Delta_{j}u\cdot\nabla]\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau

\displaystyle\triangleq\sum_{j\geq 2}B^{1}_{j}+\sum_{j\geq 2}B^{2}_{j}+\sum_{j\leq 1}A^{3}_{j}.

Using (
4.1
) and Lemma
4.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq-1}\|\Delta_{k}\sum_{j\geq 2}B^{1}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\geq-1}\sum_{j\geq\max\{2,k-4\}}2^{k}\|\Delta_{j}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\geq-1}\sum_{j\geq\max\{2,k-4\}}2^{k-j}\|\nabla\Delta_{j}u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}
