\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

For
j\geq 2
,
\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau
is supported in an annulus.
Choose a fixed annular multiplier kernel
h\in\mathcal{S}
and put

h_{j}(x)=2^{dj}h(2^{j}x)
. Then

\mathcal{R}_{i}\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau=h_{j}*\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau
and

\|h_{j}\|_{L^{1}}=\|h\|_{L^{1}}
.
Then we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq-1}\|\Delta_{k}\sum_{j\geq 2}B^{2}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

Finally, we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geq-1}\|\Delta_{k}\sum_{j\leq 1}A^{3}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\leq 4}\sum_{j\leq 1}\|\Delta_{k}A^{3}_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\leq 1}\|[\mathcal{R}_{i},\Delta_{j}u\cdot\nabla]\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\leq 1}\|\Delta_{j}u\cdot\nabla\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta_{j}u\cdot\nabla\mathcal{R}_{i}\tilde{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

We thus complete the proof of Lemma
4.2
.
∎

Corollary 4.3
.

Let
{\rm div}~u=0
and
\mathcal{R}=-(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm curl}~{\rm div}
. There exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}})(\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}).

Proof.

Since each component of
\mathcal{R}
is a finite linear combination of double Riesz transforms, it suffices to use the identity

\displaystyle[\mathcal{R}_{i}\mathcal{R}_{j},u\cdot\nabla]\tau

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{i}\mathcal{R}_{j}(u\cdot\nabla\tau)-u\cdot\nabla\left(\mathcal{R}_{i}\mathcal{R}_{j}(\tau)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{R}_{i}\left([\mathcal{R}_{j},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\right)+[\mathcal{R}_{i},u\cdot\nabla](\mathcal{R}_{j}\tau).

By virtue of Lemmas
2.8
and
4.2
, we infer that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}_{i}\left([\mathcal{R}_{j},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\right)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}_{i}\left([\mathcal{R}_{j},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\right)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Delta_{-1}[\mathcal{R}_{j},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\|[\mathcal{R}_{j},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

Similarly, we obtain

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R}_{i},u\cdot\nabla](\mathcal{R}_{j}\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\|\Delta_{-1}\mathcal{R}_{j}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}_{j}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\right)+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\left(\|\mathcal{R}_{j}\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\right)+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}})(\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}).

Thus we conclude that

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}})(\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}).

This completes the proof of Corollary
4.3
.
∎

Now, we establish global existence for (
1.3
) with low regularity.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
:

Firstly, for any
T>0
, we assume that

\|(u,\tau)\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{2}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq 4c^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Taking
L^{2}
inner product for
\eqref{eq0}_{1}
with
u
, we obtain

(4.2)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\langle{\rm div}~\tau,u\rangle.

Taking
L^{2}
inner product for
\eqref{eq0}_{2}
with
\tau
, we get

(4.3)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}-\langle Du,\tau\rangle

\displaystyle=-\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\tau\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{a}{2}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

where we take
c^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\frac{a}{100C}
.
By virtue of cancellation
\langle{\rm div}~\tau,u\rangle+\langle Du,\tau\rangle=0
, we infer from (
4.2
) and (
4.3
) that

(4.4)

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}_{T}(L^{2})}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{T}(L^{2})}+a\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{T}(L^{2})}\leq 2\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

From now on, we focus on the estimate of
\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}
. Applying Duhamel’s principle to
\eqref{eq0}_{2}
and taking
\Delta_{j}
with
j\geq 0
, we have

(4.5)

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}\tau=e^{\Delta t}e^{-at}\Delta_{j}\tau_{0}-\int_{0}^{t}e^{\Delta(t-s)}e^{-a(t-s)}\Delta_{j}(Q(\nabla u,\tau)+u\cdot\nabla\tau-Du)(s)\,ds.

Estimating (
4.5
) at the endpoint
T
, for
j\geq 0
, we infer from Lemma
2.6
that

(4.6)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}\tau(T)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{j}\tau_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}\|\Delta_{j}Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}\|\Delta_{j}(u\cdot\nabla\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}dt+\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}\|\Delta_{j}Du\|_{L^{\infty}}dt.

Here
c_{H}>0
is the heat-decay constant from Lemma
2.6
,
independent of the initial-data smallness parameter
c
.
Applying Bernstein’s inequality, we infer from (
4.4
) and (
1.10
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}\|\Delta_{j}Du\|_{L^{\infty}}dt\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}u\|_{L^{\infty}}dt\lesssim 2^{-j}\|u\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{\infty})}\lesssim 2^{-j}c^{\frac{3}{4}}.

According to Young’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}\|\Delta_{j}Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j}\|\nabla u\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{\infty})}\|\tau\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{2})}\lesssim 2^{-j}c

and

(4.7)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}\|\Delta_{j}(u\cdot\nabla\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(T-t)}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}(u\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j}\|u\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{\infty})}\|\tau\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{\infty})}\lesssim 2^{-j}c.

Combining the estimates (
4.6
)-(
4.7
), we obtain

(4.8)

\displaystyle\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{-1}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\|\Delta_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(L^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+c^{\frac{3}{4}}\lesssim c^{\frac{3}{4}}.

Moreover, we can cancel
{\rm div}~\tau
and
\Delta\tau
in (
1.3
) by virtue of the structural trick

(4.9)

\displaystyle\Gamma=\omega-\mathcal{R}\tau,

where
\mathcal{R}=-(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm curl}~{\rm div}
. Using (
4.9
) and (
1.3
), we infer that

(4.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma+u\cdot\nabla\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}\Gamma=(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u,\tau)+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau.

Apply Lemma
2.9
to
e^{t/2}\Gamma
. Choose
c
smaller,
if necessary, so that
C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq 1/4
under the bootstrap bound. Then (
4.10
) gives

\displaystyle\|\Gamma(T)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{-T/2+V(0,T)}\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{T}e^{-(T-t)/2+V(t,T)}\mathcal{F}_{\Gamma}(t)\,dt

(4.11)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{T}e^{-(T-t)/4}\mathcal{F}_{\Gamma}(t)\,dt,

where

\displaystyle V(s,t)

\displaystyle=C\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla u(r)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\,dr,

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\Gamma}(t)

\displaystyle=\|\mathcal{R}\tau(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u,\tau)(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

By virtue of Lemmas
2.8
and Corollary
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}})(\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}})\lesssim c^{\frac{3}{4}}.

Since
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
is a critical space and
Q
does not have transportation structure, product laws may require additional regularity. However, in the 2D case, we discover that
Q
can be controlled by a new estimation.
Using Lemmas
2.8
,
2.2
and
2.3
, we have

(4.12)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|T_{\nabla u}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|T_{\tau}\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|R(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|R(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{1}_{2,1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim c+c^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

According to (
4.11
)-(
4.12
), we conclude from (
4.4
) that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(B^{0}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim c^{\frac{3}{4}}.

The convolution estimates above are uniform at every endpoint
in
[0,T]
, so taking the supremum gives the stated time norms.
This together with (
4.4
) and (
4.8
) ensures that

\|(u,\tau)\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L_{T}^{\infty}(L^{2}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq 2c^{\frac{1}{2}},

after further choosing
Cc^{1/4}
sufficiently small. This
strictly improves the bootstrap bound.
According to Proposition
3.3
and the continuity method, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
. ∎

5.
Global existence and uniform vanishing damping limit in
\mathbb{R}^{2}

In this section, we first look for a global estimate for (
1.3
) in
H^{1}
. Then we can obtain the global strong solution through smallness condition in the critical Besov space. Indeed our global well-posedness results hold for all damping
a\in[0,1]
and therefore extend the work in the existing literature.

Moreover, global solutions is uniformly bounded in time, which is useful to prove that the solution of supercritical regularity is also uniformly bounded in time. The results of global existence play a crucial role in considering vanishing damping limit in the same topology for any
T>0
.

Then, by virtue of the improved Fourier splitting method, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we obtain optimal time decay rates of global solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
). The high-regularity bound
\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(1+t)^{-1}
for
d=2
does not by itself give time integrability of
\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. We introduce a novel method of high-low frequency decomposition to obtain time integrability of
\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. By virtue of the optimal decay rates and key integrability, we obtain the uniform vanishing damping limit and discover a new phenomenon for (
1.3
) that the sharp rate of uniform vanishing damping limit in
L^{2}
is related to the time decay rate in
L^{2}
.

5.1.
Global existence and vanishing damping limit

Since the global estimates are uniformly in
a
, we may drop the upper index
a
of the solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model
(
1.3
) without any confusions. We first provide the following global estimate for (
1.3
) in
H^{1}
.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
d=2
and
a\in[0,1]
. Assume
(u,\tau)
is a smooth solution of (
1.3
) with divergence-free field
u_{0}\in H^{1}
and a symmetric matrix
\tau_{0}\in H^{1}
. There exists some sufficiently small constant
\epsilon>0
such that if

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}\leq{\epsilon},

then there exist a fixed constant
\eta
, for any
t>0
, we have

(5.1)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}-\eta\langle\tau,\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{\eta}{4}\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq 0,

and

(5.2)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}}.

Proof.

We start with a
bootstrap assumption

\|(u,\tau)(t)\|_{H^{1}}\leq 2{\epsilon},\quad t\geq 0.

Testing the first equation of (
1.3
) by
u
and the second equation of (
1.3
) by
\tau
, we obtain the following energy identity:

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\tau\rangle.

In the above, we have employed the identity:

\langle u,\mathrm{div}\tau\rangle+\langle D(u),\tau\rangle=0.

Applying Sobolev’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left|\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\tau\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

Therefore,

(5.3)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{3}{4}\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

To absorb the right-hand side, we choose
\eta>0
, which will be determined later. By direct computation, we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle-\eta\tau,\nabla u\ \rangle

\displaystyle=\eta\langle\partial_{t}u,\mathrm{div}\tau\ \rangle-\eta\langle\partial_{t}\tau,\nabla u\ \rangle

\displaystyle=-\frac{\eta}{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\eta\langle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{div}\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\mathrm{div}\tau\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\eta\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q(\nabla u,\tau)-\Delta\tau,\nabla u\rangle.

Applying Sobolev’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle-\eta\tau,\nabla u\ \rangle+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\eta\langle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{div}\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\mathrm{div}\tau\rangle+\eta\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q(\nabla u,\tau)-\Delta\tau,\nabla u\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\eta\Bigg(\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{4}}+a\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{8}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\eta\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{a}{8}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}},

provided
\eta
is smaller than an absolute constant, which depends only on the coefficients in Sobolev’s inequalities.

Finally, testing the first equation of (
1.3
) by
-\Delta u
, and the second equation of (
1.3
) by
-\Delta\tau
, we obtain the following energy identity:

(5.4)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|(\nabla u,\nabla\tau)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\langle(u\cdot\nabla)\tau,\Delta\tau\rangle+\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\Delta\tau\rangle,

where we have used the fact that for
d=2

\langle(u\cdot\nabla)u,\Delta u\rangle=0.

Applying Sobolev’s inequality, we get

(5.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|(\nabla u,\nabla\tau)\right\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+a\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{4}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|u\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{8}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\left(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{4}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C{\epsilon}^{4}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C{\epsilon}^{4}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

where we have employed the bootstrap assumption in the last inequality.

Choose
0<\eta\leq 1/10
and then
{\epsilon}>0
so that

C\eta\leq 1/8
and
C({\epsilon}^{2}+{\epsilon}^{4})\leq\eta/8
.
Combining (
5.3
)-(
5.5
), we obtain the energy inequality

(5.6)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-\eta\langle\tau,\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{\eta}{4}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq 0.

When
\eta\leq\frac{1}{10}
, by (
5.6
), we obtain

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\leq\frac{3}{2}{\epsilon},

which is stronger than our bootstrap assumption at the start. Hence, (
5.1
) follows from (
5.6
) and the standard bootstrap argument.
For completeness, the sharper inequality (
5.2
) uses the
transport cancellation rather than the rough estimate in (
5.5
).
Indeed, integration by parts and the two-dimensional
Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequalities give

\displaystyle|\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau,\Delta\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{4}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{2},

\displaystyle|\langle Q,\Delta\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{2}\|\tau\|_{2}^{1/2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{2}^{3/2}.

Young’s inequality bounds their sum by

\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{2}^{2}+C\|\nabla u\|_{2}^{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{2}^{2}+C\|\nabla u\|_{2}^{4}\|\tau\|_{2}^{2}
.
The bootstrap bound
\|\nabla u\|_{2}\leq 2{\epsilon}
and
(
5.4
) now yield (
5.2
), uniformly for
a\in[0,1]
.
We finish the proof of Proposition
5.1
.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

Through the global estimates in Proposition
5.1
and compactness methods, one can infer that the equation (
1.3
) has global
H^{1}
weak solutions when
d=2
.

Since the core difficulty term
Q
exists, we cannot elevate weak solutions to strong solutions solely by improving regularity. Specifically, we fail to obtain the global estimate of
\|\omega\|_{L^{\infty}}
to prove uniqueness of the solutions, see
[
43
]
. However, we can obtain the global strong solution for
a\in[0,1]
through smallness condition in the critical Besov space. The following result of the global existence do not rely on damping and is original in the literature to our knowledge.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
d=2
and
a\in[0,1]
. Assume a divergence-free field
u_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}
and a symmetric matrix
\tau_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. There exists some positive constant
{\epsilon}
small enough such that if

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq{\epsilon},

then (
1.3
) admits a global solution
(u,\tau)
with

(u,\tau)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})
. Moreover, there exists a
C_{2}>1
such that

\|(u,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq C_{2}{\epsilon}.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.1
, we have already obtained

(5.7)

\displaystyle\left\|(u(t),\tau(t))\right\|_{H^{1}}\leq\frac{3}{2}{\epsilon},\quad t\geq 0.

Now we need to estimate

\left\|(\nabla u(t),\tau(t))\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

Estimate of
\|\tau(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
.

By Duhamel’s formula, we get

\tau(t)=e^{(\Delta-a)t}\tau_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{(\Delta-a)(t-t^{\prime})}\left[Du-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q(\nabla u,\tau)\right]dt^{\prime}.

Applying Sobolev’s inequality and Bernstein’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle=\|\Delta_{-1}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\|\Delta_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(\Delta-a)(t-t^{\prime})}\Delta_{j}\left[Du-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q(\nabla u,\tau)\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(t-t^{\prime})}2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\left(\|Du\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{8}}\|\tau\|_{L^{8}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{4}}\right)dt^{\prime}.

This together with (
5.7
) ensures that there exist a
C_{1}>1
such that

(5.8)

\displaystyle\|\tau(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{1}{\epsilon}.

The estimate for
\|\tau(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
is crucial for obtaining global strong solutions, which are time uniformly bounded.

Estimate of
\|\nabla u(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
.

We still start with a
bootstrap assumption

(5.9)

\displaystyle\|\nabla u(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{2}{\epsilon},

where
C_{2}\geq 10CC_{1}
and
{\epsilon}\leq\frac{1}{CC_{2}}
.

We now introduce the following structural variable
\Gamma
:

\Gamma=\omega-\mathcal{R}\tau.

By direct calculation,
\Gamma
satisfies the following transport equation with damping:

(5.10)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma+u\cdot\nabla\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}\Gamma=(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau+\mathcal{R}Q+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau:=F.

The structural trick
\Gamma
allows us to transfer dissipation from
\tau
to
u
, which is crucial for obtaining a closed estimate of the global solution.

By Lemma
2.5
, Lemma
2.7
, and (
5.7
), (
5.8
),

\displaystyle\|F\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathcal{R}(\tau)\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|\mathcal{R}Q\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q\|_{L^{2}}+\|Q\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2CC_{1}{\epsilon}+C_{2}{\epsilon}^{2}+CC_{2}{\epsilon}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

By the equation (
5.10
), and Proposition
5.1
, we infer that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-t^{\prime})}\|F\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim 3CC_{1}{\epsilon}+CC_{2}{\epsilon}^{2}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\omega\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq 4CC_{1}{\epsilon}+CC_{2}{\epsilon}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}C_{2}{\epsilon},

which is stronger than our bootstrap assumption (
5.9
). Hence, we obtain the uniformly bound

\|\nabla u(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{2}{\epsilon}.

We finish the proof of Proposition
5.3
.
∎

According to Proposition
5.3
, one can see that global solutions is uniformly bounded in time, which is useful to improve the regularity of the solution under additional initial conditions. The following corollary show that the solution of supercritical regularity is also uniformly bounded in time, which plays a crucial role in considering vanishing damping limit for any
T>0
.

Corollary 5.4
.

Under the conditions in Proposition
5.3
, if additionally
u_{0}\in H^{2}\cap B^{2}_{\infty,1}
and
\tau_{0}\in H^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}
, then

(u,\tau)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{2}\cap B^{2}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{2}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})
. Moreover,
there holds

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{2})}+\|(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\leq C(\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}).

Remark 5.5
.

According to the theory for the transport equation, the velocity
u\in B^{1}_{\infty,1}\hookrightarrow C^{0,1}
is the key to persisting regularity. Under the conditions in Proposition
5.3
, one can prove the global existence of smooth solutions
(u,\tau)
for the inviscid Oldroyd-B equation (
1.3
) with extra smooth initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
.

Proof.

By energy estimates for (
1.3
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla^{2}(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+a\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\langle\nabla^{2}(u\cdot\nabla u),\nabla^{2}u\rangle-\langle\nabla^{2}(u\cdot\nabla\tau),\nabla^{2}\tau\rangle-\langle\nabla^{2}Q,\nabla^{2}\tau\rangle.

By virtue of integration by parts, we infer from Proposition
5.1
that

\displaystyle|\langle-\nabla^{2}(u\cdot\nabla u),\nabla^{2}u\rangle-\langle\nabla^{2}(u\cdot\nabla\tau),\nabla^{2}\tau\rangle-\langle\nabla^{2}Q,\nabla^{2}\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle-[\nabla^{2},u\cdot\nabla]u,\nabla^{2}u\rangle+\langle\nabla(u\cdot\nabla\tau),\nabla^{3}\tau\rangle+\langle\nabla Q,\nabla^{3}\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle{\epsilon}\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+{\epsilon}\|\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+{\epsilon}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2},

Similarly, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle\eta\nabla\tau,-\nabla^{2}u\rangle+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\eta\langle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{div}~\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\Delta\mathrm{div}~\tau\rangle

\displaystyle~~~+\eta\langle\nabla(u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q-\Delta\tau),\nabla^{2}u\rangle.

Then we have

\displaystyle|\eta\langle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{div}\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\Delta\mathrm{div}\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\eta\|\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\eta\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+{\epsilon}\|\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+{\epsilon}\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

and

\displaystyle|\eta\langle\nabla(u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q-\Delta\tau),\nabla^{2}u\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\eta\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\eta a\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\eta\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle+\eta\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\eta\|\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle{\epsilon}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+{\epsilon}\|\nabla u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\eta^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Combining the above inequalities and (
5.1
), we obtain

\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle+\eta\langle\nabla\tau,-\nabla^{2}u\rangle\right)+\frac{1}{8}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{8}\|\nabla u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq 0,

which implies that for any
T>0
, we have

(5.11)

\|(u,\tau)\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(H^{2})}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}dt\leq C\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}^{2}.

We now focus on the estimate of
\|\tau\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}
. Using Lemma
2.6
, we get

(5.12)

\displaystyle\|\tau(t)\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta_{-1}\tau(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{j}\|\Delta_{j}\tau(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}2^{j}\left\|e^{(\Delta-a)(t-t^{\prime})}\Delta_{j}\left[Du-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q(\nabla u,\tau)\right]\right\|_{L^{\infty}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c_{H}2^{2j}(t-t^{\prime})}2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\left(\|u\|_{H^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{L^{4}}\right)dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}.

Using Lemma
2.4
, we have

\displaystyle\Big\|(2^{j}\|[\Delta_{j},u\cdot\nabla]\Gamma\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}})_{j}\Big\|_{l^{1}(\mathbb{Z})}\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\Gamma\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}.

This together with Proposition
5.3
implies that

(5.13)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\sup_{0\leq r\leq T}\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(r-t)}\|F(t)\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt

By Lemmas
2.5
and
2.7
, we infer from (
5.11
) and (
5.12
) that

\displaystyle\|F\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|F\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla F\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|\tau\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|Q\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|\nabla([\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau)\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|u\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle+\left\|[\mathcal{R},\nabla u\cdot\nabla]\tau\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\nabla\tau\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{B^{\frac{1}{2}}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|u\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{B^{\frac{3}{2}}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|u\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{H^{3}}.

This together with (
5.13
) ensures that
the factor
\|\tau\|_{H^{2}}
can be absorbed after convolution,
not by assuming it is pointwise small. Specifically,
(
5.1
), (
5.11
), and Cauchy–Schwarz imply

\displaystyle\sup_{r\geq 0}\int_{0}^{r}e^{-(r-t)/4}\|\tau(t)\|_{H^{2}}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}H^{1}}+C\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}\leq C{\epsilon},

\displaystyle\sup_{r\geq 0}\int_{0}^{r}e^{-(r-t)/4}\|\tau(t)\|_{H^{3}}\,dt

\displaystyle\leq C\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}.

Only
H^{2}
regularity of the initial data is used in these
convolution bounds; the additional Besov norms in the
corollary need only be finite.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\sup_{0\leq r\leq T}\int_{0}^{r}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(r-t)}(\left\|\Gamma\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{H^{2}}+\left\|u\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\left\|\tau\right\|_{H^{3}})dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+{\epsilon}\|\Gamma\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}.

This implies that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{L_{T}^{\infty}(B^{1}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{2}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}.

We thus complete the proof of Corollary
5.4
.
∎

With the results of the solutions are uniformly bounded in time, we can consider vanishing damping limit for any
T>0
under the same topology and prove vanishing damping rate for low frequency of the solutions. Since the solutions
(u,\tau)
of the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) depends on damping
a\in[0,1]
, when considering the problems of vanishing damping limit, we denote the solutions of (
1.3
) as
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
.

Proposition 5.6
.

Under the conditions in Proposition
5.3
, for any
T>0
, there holds

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}\leq C_{T}a,

and

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Proof.

Let
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
be a solution of
(
1.3
)
with the initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
and
a\in[0,1]
. Then we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}(u^{a}-u^{0})+u^{a}\cdot\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0})+\nabla(P_{u^{a}}-P_{u^{0}})\\[4.30554pt]
={\rm div}~(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})-(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla u^{0},~~~~{\rm div}~(u^{a}-u^{0})=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})+u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})-\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})+Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\\[4.30554pt]
=-a\tau^{a}+D(u^{a}-u^{0})-(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0}-Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0}),\\[4.30554pt]
(u^{a}-u^{0})|_{t=0}=0,~~(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})|_{t=0}=0.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

We divide the proof into three parts.

Step 1: Vanishing damping limit for low frequency.

By (
5.1
), we obtain the following energy identity:

(5.19)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\langle a\tau^{a}+Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})+Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0})+(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0},(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\rangle

\displaystyle-\langle\left[(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla u^{0}\right],(u^{a}-u^{0})\rangle.

Define

\displaystyle\phi(t):=\left\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\right\|_{L^{2}}.

Then we have the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\left|\langle(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla u^{0},u^{a}-u^{0}\rangle\right|\leq\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\phi^{2}(t),

and

\displaystyle\left|\langle a\tau^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\rangle\right|\leq a^{2}+\|\tau^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\phi^{2}(t).

One can infer that

\displaystyle\left|\langle Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}),\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\rangle\right|\leq\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\phi^{2}(t),

and

\displaystyle\left|\langle Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0}),\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\phi^{2}(t)\left(\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{4/3}+\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right).

Moreover, we obtain

(5.20)

\displaystyle\left|\langle(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0},(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\phi^{2}(t)\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{4/3}.

Here
\|\Sigma\|_{4}\lesssim\|\Sigma\|_{2}^{1/2}\|\nabla\Sigma\|_{2}^{1/2}
, for
\Sigma=\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}
.
No
L^{4}
assumption on
\nabla\tau_{0}
is needed:

\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{4}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau^{0}\|_{2}
, so its
4/3
power is
integrable on every finite interval by (
5.1
).
Applying Gronwall’s inequality, we infer from (
5.19
)-(
5.20
) that

(5.21)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\phi^{2}(t)\lesssim_{T}a^{2}.

Next we estimate
\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. Note that

(5.22)

\displaystyle\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle=\|\Delta_{-1}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\|\Delta_{j}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\|\Delta_{j}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}a+\sum_{j\geq 0}\|\Delta_{j}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}.

By Duhamel’s formula

\displaystyle\Delta_{j}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{t}e^{\Delta(t-s)}\Delta_{j}\bigg[D(u^{a}-u^{0})-u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})

\displaystyle\quad-(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0}-Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})-Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0})-a\tau^{a}\bigg]ds.

Applying Lemma
2.6
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}\Bigg[\|\Delta_{j}D(u^{a}-u^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Delta_{j}\left[u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\right]\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\Delta_{j}\left[(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0}\right]\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Delta_{j}Q(\nabla u^{a},(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}))\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\quad+\|\Delta_{j}Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}+a\|\Delta_{j}\tau^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\Bigg]ds

\displaystyle=A_{1}+A_{2}+A_{3}+A_{4}+A_{5}+A_{6}.

Applying Bernstein’s inequality and Proposition
5.3
, we have the following inequalities:

\displaystyle A_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{4}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}^{\frac{1}{4}}ds\lesssim_{T}2^{-\frac{j}{2}}a^{\frac{3}{4}},

and

\displaystyle A_{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{7}{4}j}\|u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\frac{8}{3}}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{7}{4}j}\|u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{4}}ds\lesssim_{T}2^{-\frac{j}{4}}a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

One can deduce that

\displaystyle A_{3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{4}}\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}^{\frac{1}{4}}\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}ds\lesssim_{T}a^{\frac{3}{4}}2^{-\frac{j}{2}},

and

\displaystyle A_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{j}\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}ds\lesssim_{T}2^{-j}a.

For
A_{5}
, put
U=u^{a}-u^{0}
and integrate the derivative in

Q(\nabla U,\tau^{0})
by parts: each component is a sum of

\partial_{k}(U_{\ell}\tau^{0}_{mn})
and

U_{\ell}\partial_{k}\tau^{0}_{mn}
. Thus

\displaystyle A_{5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}\left[2^{3j/2}\|U\|_{4}\|\tau^{0}\|_{\infty}+2^{j}\|U\|_{4}\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{4}\right]ds

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}2^{-j/2}a^{3/4}.

In the second term use

\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}(0,T;L^{4})}\leq C_{T}
and the

L^{4/3}
norm
C2^{-3j/2}
of the heat kernel in time.
The interpolation
\|U\|_{4}\lesssim\|U\|_{2}^{3/4}\|U\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}^{1/4}\lesssim_{T}a^{3/4}

controls both terms.
and

\displaystyle A_{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)2^{2j}}a\|\tau^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}ds\lesssim 2^{-2j}a.

The above inequalities together with (
5.22
) ensure that

(5.23)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim_{T}a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

Step 2: Vanishing damping limit in
\dot{H}^{1}
.

In general analysis for stability and vanishing limit, there is a phenomenon of supercritical regularity in the estimate of transport terms
u^{a}\cdot\nabla u^{a}
. We introduce a low-frequency truncated smoothing operator
S_{N}
to quantify the regularity.

Let
(u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})
be a solution of
(
1.3
)
with the initial data
(S_{N}u_{0},S_{N}\tau_{0})
and
a\in[0,1]
. Then we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})+u^{a}\cdot\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})+\nabla(P_{u^{a}}-P_{u_{N}^{a}})\\[4.30554pt]
={\rm div}~(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})-(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla u_{N}^{a},~~~~{\rm div}~(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})+u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})-\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})+a(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})+Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})\\[4.30554pt]
=D(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})-(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}-Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}),\tau_{N}^{a}),\\[4.30554pt]
(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})|_{t=0}=(Id-S_{N})u_{0},~~(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})|_{t=0}=(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Proceeding
L^{2}
energy estimate again, we obtain

(5.29)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0},(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0},(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{H^{1}}.

For these fixed initial data, define the tail

c_{N}=\|(Id-S_{N})(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\longrightarrow 0.

All constants below are independent of
a
and
N
; the notation

c_{N}
in bounds may absorb a fixed multiple of this tail.
Then (
5.29
) can be simplified by

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}.

Next, we test the first equation of (
5.1
) by
-\Delta(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})
and the second equation of (
5.1
) by
-\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})
, we obtain the following energy inequality:

(5.30)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}),\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\left\|\nabla^{2}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\langle u^{a}\cdot\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}),\Delta(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\rangle\right|+\left|\langle(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\cdot\nabla u^{a}_{N},\Delta(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\langle u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}),\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\rangle\right|+\left|\langle(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\cdot\nabla\tau^{a}_{N},\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\langle Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}),\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\rangle\right|+\left|\langle Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}),\tau^{a}_{N}),\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle B_{1}+B_{2}+B_{3}+B_{4}+B_{5}+B_{6}.

By virtue of Corollary
5.4
and integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle B_{1}+B_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\left(\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}c_{N}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}\left(\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}+1\right)\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c^{2}_{N}.

By Hölder’s inequality and Corollary
5.4
,

\displaystyle B_{3}+B_{4}+B_{5}+B_{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{2}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{T}

\displaystyle\|u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{2}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+c^{2}_{N}.

By the above inequalities, applying Gronwall’s inequality to (
5.30
), we have

(5.31)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}),\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{T}c_{N}.

According to (
1.3
), we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})+u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})+\nabla(P_{u_{N}^{a}}-P_{u_{N}^{0}})\\[4.30554pt]
={\rm div}~(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})-(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\cdot\nabla u_{N}^{0},~~~~{\rm div}~(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})+u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})-\Delta(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})+Q(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\\[4.30554pt]
=-a\tau_{N}^{a}+D(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})-(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}^{0}-Q(\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0}),\tau_{N}^{0}),\\[4.30554pt]
(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})|_{t=0}=0,~~(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})|_{t=0}=0.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Testing the first equation of (
5.1
) by
-\Delta(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N})
, the second equation by
-\Delta(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})
, and integrating by parts, we have the following energy inequality:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}{\underbrace{\left\|\nabla(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}),\nabla(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}_{\coloneqq M_{1}(t)}}+\left\|\nabla^{2}(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\langle u^{a}_{N}\cdot\nabla(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}),\Delta(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N})\rangle\right|+\left|\langle(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N})\cdot\nabla u^{0}_{N},\Delta(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\langle a\tau^{a}_{N},\Delta(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\rangle\right|+\left|\langle u^{a}_{N}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N}),\Delta(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\langle(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0}_{N},\Delta(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\rangle\right|+\left|\langle Q(\nabla u^{a}_{N},\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N}),\Delta(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\langle Q(\nabla(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}),\tau^{0}_{N}),\Delta(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\|\nabla u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\right)M_{1}(t)+\|u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{2}(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+a^{2}\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}M_{1}(t)

\displaystyle+\|u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\nabla u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}M_{1}(t).

By the same argument in
Step 1
, we have

\displaystyle\|(u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N},\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{T}a.

By the virtue of Corollary
5.4
, we get

\displaystyle\|\nabla^{2}u^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 2^{N}.

Applying Gronwall’s inequality, we have

(5.37)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}M_{1}(t)\lesssim_{T}a^{2}2^{2N}.

Combining with (
5.31
) and (
5.37
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{T}c_{N}+a2^{N},

which implies that

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1})\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1})}=0.

Step 3: Vanishing damping limit in
B^{1}_{\infty,1}
.

We also need a low-frequency truncated smoothing operator
S_{N}
to quantify the regularity. In addition, we also need to establish a low-frequency analysis of the global stability for
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
regarding the smoothing operator
S_{N}
.
Define

\begin{cases}M_{2}(t)\coloneqq\left\|(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})(t)\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}},\\
M_{3}(t)\coloneqq\left\|(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})(t)\right\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\right\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}ds.\end{cases}

By the first equation of (
5.1
), we infer from Proposition
5.3
that

(5.38)

\displaystyle M_{2}(t)\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\cdot\nabla u^{a}_{N}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla(p_{u^{a}}-p_{u^{a}_{N}})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds+M_{3}(t).

Applying Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\cdot\nabla u^{a}_{N}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}M_{2}(s)ds.

By the virtue of Lemma
3.1
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla(p_{u^{a}}-p_{u^{a}_{N}})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm div}\Big[u^{a}\cdot\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})+(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\cdot\nabla u^{a}_{N}\Big]\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\nabla(-\Delta)^{-1}\mathrm{div}\mathrm{div}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}M_{2}(s)ds+M_{3}(t)+2^{-N}c_{N}.

These together with (
5.38
) ensure that

(5.39)

\displaystyle M_{2}(t)\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}+\int_{0}^{t}M_{2}(s)ds+M_{3}(t).

Write
\Theta_{N}=\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a}
. By (
5.29
),

\|\Delta_{-1}\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{\infty})}+\|\Delta_{-1}\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{1}(0,T;L^{\infty})}\leq C_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}.

For the blocks
j\geq 0
, the heat estimate has a constant
independent of
T
. Consequently, for
0\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle M_{3}(t)\leq{}

\displaystyle C_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{a}\cdot\nabla\Theta_{N}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}\,ds

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\|(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}\,ds

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\|Q(\nabla u^{a},\Theta_{N})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}\,ds

\displaystyle+C\int_{0}^{t}\|Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}),\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}\,ds+C\int_{0}^{t}M_{2}(s)\,ds,

where
C
is independent of
a
,
N
, and
T
.

By the transport structure and Lemma
2.5
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\cdot\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}ds\lesssim\epsilon M_{3}(t).

By Lemma
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\cdot\nabla\tau^{a}_{N}\right\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\left\|u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{H^{2}}ds.

By virtue of Bony’s decomposition, we infer that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}ds\lesssim{\epsilon}M_{3}(t),

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\left\|Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}),\tau^{a}_{N})\right\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}M_{2}(s)\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{H^{2}}ds.

Then we get

(5.40)

\displaystyle M_{3}(t)\leq C_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}+C\int_{0}^{t}M_{2}(s)(1+\|\tau_{N}^{a}(s)\|_{H^{2}})\,ds.

Combining (
5.39
) and (
5.40
), and applying Gronwall’s inequality, we obtain

(5.41)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left[M_{2}(t)+M_{3}(t)\right]\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}.

The coefficient in this Gronwall estimate is uniform in
N
:
(
5.1
) gives

\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{2})}\leq C_{T}{\epsilon}
.
Moreover, writing
\Theta_{N}=\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a}
, interpolation yields

(5.42)

\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}^{1/2}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{1}(0,T;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}^{1/2}\leq C_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}.

Consequently products of this error with
\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{H^{2}}

are integrable with bound
C_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}
, without any
smallness assumption on the high norms of the truncated data.
Recall that

(5.43)

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\Gamma^{a}=\omega^{a}-\mathcal{R}\tau^{a},\\
\Gamma^{a}_{N}=\omega^{a}_{N}-\mathcal{R}\tau^{a}_{N}.\end{cases}

According to (
1.3
) and (
5.43
), we infer that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{a})+u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{a})+\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{a})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{N}^{a}+(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})+[\mathcal{R},u^{a}\cdot\nabla](\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})+[\mathcal{R},(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{a}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}),\tau_{N}^{a}).

Define
M_{4}(t):=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

By the standard estimate for the transport equation, we have

(5.44)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle e^{-\frac{1}{4}t}c_{N}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{a}_{N}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|[\mathcal{R},u^{a}\cdot\nabla](\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|[\mathcal{R},(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{a}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}),\tau_{N}^{a})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds=:e^{-\frac{1}{4}t}c_{N}+C_{1}+C_{2}+C_{3}+C_{4}+C_{5}+C_{6}.

By Corollary
5.4
and (
5.41
), we deduce that

\displaystyle C_{1}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\Gamma^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}ds\lesssim_{T}c_{N}.

By the property of the Calderon-Zygmund operator, we have

\displaystyle C_{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left(\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\right)ds\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}.

By Corollary
4.3
, there holds

\displaystyle C_{3}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left(\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u^{a}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\right)ds\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}.

Applying Corollary
4.3
again, we get

\displaystyle C_{4}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left(\|\omega^{a}-\omega^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\right)ds\lesssim{\epsilon}M_{4}(t)+C_{T}c_{N},

where we have used the fact that

\displaystyle\|\omega^{a}-\omega^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\mathcal{R}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

By Lemma
2.5
and (
5.41
), we obtain

\displaystyle C_{5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\left(\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\right)ds\lesssim_{T}2^{-N}c_{N}.

Now we deal with
C_{6}
:

\displaystyle C_{6}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}),\tau_{N}^{a})\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}),\tau_{N}^{a})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds=:C_{61}+C_{62}.

C_{61}
can be estimated directly by virtue of (
5.31
),

\displaystyle C_{61}\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}ds\lesssim_{T}c_{N}.

For the term
C_{62}
, applying Lemma
2.5
,

\displaystyle C_{62}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\nabla(u^{a}-u^{a}_{N})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{H^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left(\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u^{a}-u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathcal{R}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{a}_{N})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\right)\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{H^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}M_{4}(t)+C_{T}2^{-N}c_{N},

where we have used Proposition
5.1
. Due to (
5.44
), we infer that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{a}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim_{T}c_{N}.

Finally, we estimate
\|\Gamma^{a}_{N}-\Gamma^{0}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. According to (
1.3
), we infer that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(\Gamma_{N}^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{0})+u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla(\Gamma_{N}^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{0})+\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma_{N}^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{N}^{0}-\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{R}(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})+a\mathcal{R}\tau_{N}^{a}

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{R},u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla](\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})+[\mathcal{R},(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{0}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0}),\tau_{N}^{0}).

Define
M_{5}(t):=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t}\|(\Gamma^{a}_{N}-\Gamma^{0}_{N})(s)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

By the estimate for the transport equations, we have

(5.45)

\displaystyle\|(\Gamma^{a}_{N}-\Gamma^{0}_{N})(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{N}^{0}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}a\left\|\mathcal{R}\tau_{N}^{a}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|[\mathcal{R},u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla](\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|[\mathcal{R},(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{0}\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0}),\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds=:\sum_{j=1}^{7}D_{j}.

By (
5.21
), Lemma
2.5
and Corollary
5.4
, we infer that

\displaystyle D_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\Gamma^{0}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\Gamma^{0}_{N}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}ds\lesssim_{T}a^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{N}.

By the property of the Calderon-Zygmund operator, (
5.21
) and (
5.23
), we get

\displaystyle D_{2}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left(\|\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\right)ds\lesssim_{T}a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

By the embedding
H^{2}\hookrightarrow B^{0}_{\infty,1}
and Proposition
5.1
,

D_{3}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}a\|\tau^{a}_{N}\|_{H^{2}}ds\lesssim a.

Applying Corollary
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle D_{4}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left(\|\nabla u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u^{a}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\left(\|\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\right)ds\lesssim_{T}a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

Applying Lemma
2.7
, we deduce that

\displaystyle D_{5}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\omega^{a}_{N}-\omega^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}\cap L^{2}}\|\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{H^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left(\|\Gamma^{a}_{N}-\Gamma^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\mathcal{R}(\tau^{a}_{N}-\tau^{0}_{N})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\omega^{a}_{N}-\omega^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\right)\|\tau^{0}_{N}\|_{H^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}M_{5}(t)+C_{T}\left(a^{\frac{3}{4}}+a2^{N}\right).

We divide the estimate of
D_{6}
into two parts:

\displaystyle D_{6}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|Q(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|Q(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds=:D_{61}+D_{62}.

By (
5.21
), we get

\displaystyle D_{61}\leq\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0}\|_{L^{2}}ds\lesssim_{T}a.

By virtue of Bony’s decomposition, we have

\displaystyle D_{62}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\left(\|\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0}\|_{H^{1}}\right)ds\lesssim_{T}a^{\frac{3}{4}}+a2^{N}.

We also divide the estimate of
D_{7}
into two parts:

\displaystyle D_{7}\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0}),\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{L^{2}}ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left\|Q(\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0}),\tau_{N}^{0})\right\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds=:D_{71}+D_{72}.

By (
5.37
), we obtain

\displaystyle D_{71}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau_{N}^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}ds\lesssim_{T}a2^{N}.

For the term
D_{72}
, applying Lemma
2.5
,

\displaystyle D_{72}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|\nabla(u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau_{N}^{0}\|_{H^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\left[\|\Gamma_{N}^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\mathcal{R}(\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|u^{a}_{N}-u^{0}_{N}\|_{L^{2}}\right]\|\tau_{N}^{0}\|_{H^{2}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}M_{5}(t)+C_{T}a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

Recall (
5.45
), we infer that

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}M_{5}(t)\lesssim_{T}a^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{N}.

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim_{T}c_{N}+a^{\frac{1}{2}}2^{N}.

This implies that

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}=0.

We thus complete the proof of Proposition
5.6
.
∎

5.2.
The key integrability and uniform vanishing damping limit

By virtue of the improved Fourier splitting method, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we obtain optimal time decay rates of global solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model. However, we fail to obtain the decay rates of
\|(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
in the case of critical regularity. The high-regularity bound
\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(1+t)^{-1}
for
d=2
is borderline and alone does not imply time integrability of this norm. We introduce a different method of high-low frequency decomposition to obtain time integrability of
\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Under the conditions in Proposition
5.3
, if additionally
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in\dot{B}^{-1}_{2,\infty},

then there holds

\|(u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+(1+t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

and

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt\leq C_{0},

where
C_{0}
depends on
\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}\cap\dot{B}^{-1}_{2,\infty}}
and
\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

Proof.

We first prove the decay estimate with constants independent of
a
.
Write

E=\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}
,

Y=\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|_{2}^{2}
,

D=\|\nabla u\|_{2}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}
, and

\mathscr{E}=E-2\eta\langle\tau,\nabla u\rangle\sim E
.
By (
5.1
),

\mathscr{E}^{\prime}+cD\leq 0
.
Let
L(r,t)=\int_{|\xi|\leq r}(|\hat{u}|^{2}+|\hat{\tau}|^{2})\,d\xi

and
A=\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{\dot{B}^{-1}_{2,\infty}}^{2}
.
The Fourier energy identity, symmetry of
\tau
, and

\operatorname{div}u=0
give, for
0<r\leq 1
,

(5.46)

L(r,t)\leq Cr^{2}\left(A+\int_{0}^{t}E(s)^{3/2}\,ds\right)+Cr\int_{0}^{t}E(s)D(s)^{1/2}\,ds.

For example the transport terms are divergences, hence their
contribution is bounded by

Cr^{2}(\|u\|_{2}+\|\tau\|_{2})^{3}
.
For
Q
use
\|\widehat{Q}\|_{\infty}\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{2}\|\tau\|_{2}
and

\|\hat{\tau}\|_{L^{1}(|\xi|\leq r)}\leq Cr\|\tau\|_{2}
.
The initial term is bounded by
Cr^{2}A
by a homogeneous dyadic sum.
The damping term has favorable sign and is discarded.
Fourier splitting therefore yields

(5.47)

\mathscr{E}^{\prime}+cr(t)^{2}\mathscr{E}+\tfrac{c}{2}D\leq Cr(t)^{2}L(r(t),t).

The constants and the choice of
\eta
are independent of
a
;
any initial interval on which
r(t)>1
is absorbed into the constants.

We spell out the borderline bootstrap underlying this estimate
(cf. the Fourier-splitting approach in
[
16
]
).
First take
r(t)^{2}=K/((e+t)\log(e+t))
.
Since
\int_{0}^{\infty}D<\infty
, (
5.46
) gives

L\leq C\log(e+t)^{-1/2}
and thus

E\leq C\log(e+t)^{-1/2}
by (
5.47
), choosing
K
large.
If
E\leq C_{k}\log(e+t)^{-k}
, the two integrals in
(
5.46
) are bounded by

C_{k}(1+t)\log(e+t)^{-3k/2}
and

C_{k}(1+t)^{1/2}\log(e+t)^{-k}
, respectively.
The same splitting improves
k
to
k+1/2
.
Consequently, for every
m>0
,

(5.48)

E(t)\leq C_{m}\log(e+t)^{-m},\qquad\int_{0}^{\infty}\log(e+t)^{m}D(t)\,dt\leq C_{m}.

The second assertion follows by weighting

\mathscr{E}^{\prime}+cD\leq 0
and using the first assertion with exponent
m+1
.

Next take
r(t)^{2}=K/(1+t)
and integrate (
5.47
).
Using (
5.46
), one obtains

E(t)\leq\frac{C}{1+t}\left(A+\int_{0}^{t}E^{3/2}\,ds\right)+\frac{C}{(1+t)^{1/2}}\int_{0}^{t}ED^{1/2}\,ds.

For
Z(t)=\sup_{s\leq t}(1+s)^{1/2}E(s)
, this implies

Z(t)\leq C+C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{\log(e+s)^{-2}}{1+s}+\frac{D(s)^{1/2}}{(1+s)^{1/2}}\right)Z(s)\,ds.

Both coefficients are integrable by (
5.48
)
and Cauchy–Schwarz. Thus
E\leq C(1+t)^{-1/2}
.
Weighting the basic energy and (
5.2
) now gives

\int_{0}^{t}(1+s)^{3/2}D(s)\,ds\leq C(1+t),\qquad Y(t)\leq C(1+t)^{-1},\qquad\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+s)^{\beta}D(s)\,ds<\infty\quad(\beta<1/2).

Indeed
((1+t)^{2}Y)^{\prime}\leq C(1+t)^{3/2}Y
after discarding
the stress dissipation, so the middle bound follows from the first one.

An intermediate negative index removes the remaining borderline.
Define

M(t)=\sup_{s\leq t}\|(u,\tau)(s)\|_{\dot{B}^{-1/2}_{2,\infty}}.

The block energy inequality, integrating the stress transport
term by parts and absorbing it into the heat dissipation, yields

M(t)^{2}\leq CM(0)^{2}+C\int_{0}^{t}\|u\otimes\tau\|_{\dot{B}^{-1/2}_{2,\infty}}^{2}\,ds+CM(t)\int_{0}^{t}\|(u\cdot\nabla u,Q)\|_{\dot{B}^{-1/2}_{2,\infty}}\,ds.

Using the embedding of
L^{4/3}
into

\dot{B}^{-1/2}_{2,\infty}
and two-dimensional interpolation,
the two integrands have the bounds

CE^{3/2}Y^{1/2},\qquad CE^{1/4}Y^{1/4}D^{1/2}.

The first is
O((1+t)^{-5/4})
; the second is integrable by
Cauchy–Schwarz with weight
(1+t)^{3/8}D
.
Thus
M(t)\leq C
.
In the
Q
contribution to (
5.46
) we may now replace

r\|\tau\|_{2}
by

Cr\,L_{\tau}(r,s)^{1/2}\leq Cr^{3/2}M(s)
.
It follows, using
\beta=3/8
, that

L(K^{1/2}(1+t)^{-1/2},t)\leq C(1+t)^{-11/16}
.
Another application of (
5.47
) gives

E\leq C(1+t)^{-11/16}
and

\int_{0}^{\infty}(1+t)^{5/8}D\,dt<\infty
.
Hence

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(-u\cdot\nabla u,-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q)\|_{L^{1}}\,dt\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}E^{1/2}D^{1/2}\,dt<\infty,

since
(11/16)+(5/8)>1
.
The dyadic energy inequality and

L^{1}\hookrightarrow\dot{B}^{-1}_{2,\infty}
then give a uniform
bound on
\|(u,\tau)\|_{\dot{B}^{-1}_{2,\infty}}
.
Now
L(r,t)\leq Cr^{2}
; (
5.47
) gives

E\leq C(1+t)^{-1}
.
Finally

\int_{0}^{t}(1+s)^{2}D(s)\,ds\leq C(1+t)
and

((1+t)^{3}Y)^{\prime}\leq C(1+t)^{2}Y
from (
5.2
)
imply
Y\leq C(1+t)^{-2}
.
We have proved, uniformly for
a\in[0,1]
,

(5.49)

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+(1+t)^{1/2}\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{-1/2}.

Multiplying
(1+t)^{1-\delta}
with
\delta\in(0,1)
to (
5.1
) and integrating, we infer from (
5.49
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{1-\delta}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+(1+t)^{1-\delta}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{-\delta}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{-\delta-1}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}\frac{1}{\delta}.

Combining (
5.49
) and (
5.2
), we get

(5.50)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{2-\delta}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+(1+t)^{2-\delta}\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{1-\delta}\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{2-\delta}\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{-\delta-1}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}\frac{1}{\delta}.

The high-regularity bound
\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(1+t)^{-1}
for
d=2
does not by itself give time integrability of
\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
. The core difficulty lies in the low-frequency decay rate of
\tau
. We now introduce a new method to get the following key integrability:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\leq C_{0}.

We first deal with high-frequency case.
According to (
5.50
), for any
t>0
, we infer that

(5.51)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-j}\|\Delta_{j}\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim C_{0},

This means that the high frequency of
\tau
is integrable. With this information, we can study the integrability of high frequency for
\nabla u
. We deduce from the equation of
\Gamma
that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma+(u\cdot\nabla)(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(a-\frac{1}{2})(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}\tau+(Id-\Delta_{-1})[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau

\displaystyle+(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}Q-[(Id-\Delta_{-1}),(u\cdot\nabla)]\Gamma.

where
\Gamma=\omega-\mathcal{R}\tau
.
Then by Proposition
5.3
and Lemma
2.9
, we have

\displaystyle\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}t}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-t^{\prime})}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-t^{\prime})}\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\mathcal{R}Q\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|[\Delta_{-1},(u\cdot\nabla)]\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}.

This together with (
5.49
)-(
5.51
), Lemmas
2.8
,
2.5
and
2.7
ensures that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\mathcal{R}Q\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}\|[\Delta_{-1},(u\cdot\nabla)]\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\omega\|_{L^{2}})(\|\tau\|_{B^{\frac{1}{4}}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta_{-1}(u\cdot\nabla\Gamma)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|(u\cdot\nabla)\Delta_{-1}\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}(\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}.

For the last absorption, separate the low-frequency part:

\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{2}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-1}
.
Its product with
\|u\|_{2}+\|\tau\|_{2}
is integrable.
The remaining part is bounded by

C\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
;
use (
5.51
) and the small critical bound to absorb the
first term. Thus this argument does not use the low-frequency
integrability that will be proved next.
Then we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}.

Then we deal with low-frequency case. Since low-frequency operator
\Delta_{-1}
have a smoothing effect, we can consider the time weighted integrability of solutions with high-order regularity. From (
1.3
), we deduce that

(5.52)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle\Delta_{-1}\nabla\tau,-\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\langle\mathbb{P}\Delta_{-1}\left(\mathrm{div}~\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\Delta_{-1}\Delta\mathrm{div}~\tau\rangle

\displaystyle~~~+\langle\Delta_{-1}\nabla(u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q-\Delta\tau),\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\rangle.

Then we have

\displaystyle|\langle\mathbb{P}\Delta_{-1}\left(\mathrm{div}~\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\Delta_{-1}\Delta\mathrm{div}~\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

and

\displaystyle|\langle\Delta_{-1}\nabla(u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q-\Delta\tau),\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{2}}+a\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{3}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}).

Multiplying
(1+t)^{3}
to (
5.52
), then we infer from (
5.49
) that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}[(1+t)^{3}\langle\Delta_{-1}\nabla\tau,-\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\rangle]+\frac{1}{4}(1+t)^{3}\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+(1+t)^{3}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+(1+t)^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}+(1+t)^{2}|\langle\Delta_{-1}\nabla\tau,-\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\rangle|.

This together with (
5.52
) and (
5.2
) ensures that

(5.53)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{3}\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)+\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{3}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C_{0}(1+t^{\prime})^{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~+(1+t)^{3}|\langle\Delta_{-1}\nabla\tau,-\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\rangle|

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{2}|\langle\Delta_{-1}\nabla\tau,-\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\rangle|dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)+C_{0}(1+t)\left(\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{3}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{\frac{3}{2}}.

Our goal is to prove that

(5.54)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}dt\lesssim C_{0}.

By Proposition
5.3
, we only need to show that

\int_{1}^{\infty}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}dt\lesssim C_{0}.

Applying Bernstein’s inequality and Sobolev’s inequality, we infer that

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u\right\|_{L^{\infty}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla u\right\|_{L^{4}}dt\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}dt.

Note that there is time growth on the right side of the inequality (
5.53
). We now introduce a novel inequality to control time growth. By Lebesgue’s monotone convergence theorem and Hölder’s inequality, we deduce from (
5.49
) and (
5.53
) that

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{\infty}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}dt

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}\left\|\nabla u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C_{0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{j}{2}}\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{\frac{1}{2}}dt

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{j}{2}}\left(\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}(1+t)^{3}\left\|\Delta_{-1}\nabla^{2}u\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\left(\int_{2^{j}}^{2^{j+1}}(1+t)^{-1}dt\right)^{\frac{3}{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle C_{0}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-\frac{j}{8}}\lesssim C_{0}.

This implies that (
5.54
) is valid. We thus finish the proof of Proposition
5.7
.
∎

By virtue of the optimal decay rates and key integrability in the Proposition
5.7
, for
d=2
, we finally obtain the uniform vanishing damping limit in the following proposition. In addition, we discover a new phenomenon for (
1.3
) that the rate of uniform vanishing damping limit in
L^{2}
is related to the time decay rate in
L^{2}
.

Proposition 5.8
.

Under the conditions in Proposition
5.7
, there holds

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2})}\leq C_{0}a^{\frac{1}{2}},

and

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Proof.

Let
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
be a solution of
(
1.3
)
with the initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
and
a\in[0,1]
. Then we get

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}(u^{a}-u^{0})+u^{a}\cdot\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0})+\nabla(P_{u^{a}}-P_{u^{0}})\\[4.30554pt]
={\rm div}~(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})-(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla u^{0},~~~~{\rm div}~(u^{a}-u^{0})=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})+u^{a}\cdot\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})-\Delta(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})+Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\\[4.30554pt]
=-a\tau^{a}+D(u^{a}-u^{0})-(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0}-Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0}),\\[4.30554pt]
(u^{a}-u^{0})|_{t=0}=0,~~(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})|_{t=0}=0.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Step 1:
L^{2}
convergence.

Testing the first equation of (
5.2
) by
(u^{a}-u^{0})
and the second equation of (
5.2
) by
(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})
, we obtain the following energy identity:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\langle(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla u^{0},u^{a}-u^{0}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad-\langle a\tau^{a}+Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})+Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0})+(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\rangle.

Define
\phi(t):=\left\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\right\|_{L^{2}}
. We have the following inequalities:

\displaystyle\left|\langle(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla u^{0},(u^{a}-u^{0})\rangle\right|\leq\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\phi^{2}(t),

and

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}a\tau^{a}:(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})dx\right|\leq a\|\tau^{a}\|_{L^{2}}\phi(t)\lesssim aC_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi(t).

One can deduce that

\displaystyle\left|\langle Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}),\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\rangle\right|\leq\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\phi^{2}(t),

and

\displaystyle\left|\langle Q(\nabla(u^{a}-u^{0}),\tau^{0}),\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}\left(\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle+C\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\frac{1}{8}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right)\phi^{2}(t)+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\langle(u^{a}-u^{0})\cdot\nabla\tau^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u^{a}-u^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\right)\phi^{2}(t)+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla(\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Then, we deduce that

(5.60)

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}(t)\lesssim\psi(t)\phi(t)+aC_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}},

where we define
\psi(t)
as follows:

\displaystyle\psi(t)=\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

Applying (
5.50
), Proposition
5.7
