and the following interpolation inequalities for
d=2
:

\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla^{2}\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},\quad\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}\lesssim\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\|\nabla^{2}\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2})},

we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\right)dt\lesssim C_{0}.

Applying Gronwall’s inequality, we infer from (
5.60
) that

\displaystyle\phi(t)\lesssim aC_{0}(1+t)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

If
at\leq 1
, then we get
\phi(t)\leq C_{0}a^{\frac{1}{2}}
.
If
at\geq 1
, applying Proposition
5.7
, we also deduce that

\displaystyle\phi(t)\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\leq C_{0}a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 2: High-order convergence.

We need to show that: For any
0<\delta<1
, there exists a positive number
a_{0}
such that

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim\delta,

whenever
a\leq a_{0}
.

Firstly, we claim that for above
\delta>0
, by Proposition
5.7
, there exists
T^{*}\in(0,\infty)
independent with
a\in[0,1]
, such that

(5.61)

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\delta,\quad\text{for all}\ a\in[0,1],~t\geq T^{*}.

Indeed, by (
5.49
), there exists
T>0
, which is independent with
a\in[0,1]
, such that

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{H^{1}\times H^{1}}\leq\frac{\delta}{2},\quad\text{for all}\ a\in[0,1],t\geq T.

By Proposition
5.7
and (
5.50
), there exists
M>0
such that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}^{2}\right)dt\leq M.

To justify the stress contribution, the low-frequency Bernstein
and interpolation inequalities give

\|\Delta_{-1}\tau\|_{\infty}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{4}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{2}^{1/2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{2}^{1/2}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-3/4}
,
whereas

\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{2}
.
Their squares are integrable by (
5.49
) and (
5.50
).
Choose
\delta
so small that the fixed multiple
C\delta

is below the small-data threshold.
By mean-value theorem, for any
a\in[0,1]
, there exists
T_{a}\in[T,T+4M\delta^{-2}]
such that

\|\nabla u^{a}(T_{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau^{a}(T_{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}^{2}\leq\frac{\delta^{2}}{4}.

Then we have

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})(T_{a})\|_{H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\delta.

Applying the result of global well-posedness in Proposition
5.3
,

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}\times H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\delta,\quad\text{for all}\ t\geq T_{a}.

Taking
T^{*}=T+4M\delta^{-2}
, we finish the proof of the claim (
5.61
).

By Proposition
5.6
, there exists
a_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T^{*};\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,T^{*};\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq\delta,

whenever
a\leq a_{0}
.
This together with the claim (
5.61
) and triangle inequality ensures that

\displaystyle\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\lesssim\delta.

We finish the proof of Proposition
5.8
.
∎

We show that the vanishing damping rate in Proposition
5.8
is sharp in the following sense.

Proposition 5.9

(Sharpness of the vanishing damping rate)
.

There exists initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
satisfying the conditions of Proposition
5.8
such that the following estimates hold:

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2})}\gtrsim a^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Proof.

We give an exact solution, which also works in dimension
d=3
.
Choose a real Schwartz function
\phi
with

\widehat{\phi}\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\widehat{\phi}(\xi)=1
for
|\xi|\leq 1
, and set

u_{0}=0
,
\tau_{0}={\epsilon}\phi I_{d}
.
These data are symmetric, satisfy all the required Besov and
Sobolev conditions, and satisfy the small-data hypothesis when

{\epsilon}>0
is sufficiently small. In particular

2^{-dj/2}\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}\phi\|_{2}\leq C
for
j\leq 0
,
which verifies the endpoint negative Besov condition.

For every
a\in[0,1]
the solution is

(5.62)

u^{a}=0,\qquad\tau^{a}=f^{a}I_{d},\qquad P^{a}=f^{a},\qquad f^{a}={\epsilon}e^{-at}e^{t\Delta}\phi.

Indeed,
\operatorname{div}(f^{a}I_{d})=\nabla f^{a}
is balanced by
the pressure, while
D(u^{a})=0
and
Q(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})=0

for every
b\in[-1,1]
. Thus no nonlinear remainder occurs.
By Plancherel’s identity, for
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\|\tau^{a}(t)-\tau^{0}(t)\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{d}\,{\epsilon}(1-e^{-at})\|e^{t\Delta}\phi\|_{2}

\displaystyle\geq c_{d}{\epsilon}(1-e^{-at})\left(\int_{|\xi|\leq t^{-1/2}}e^{-2t|\xi|^{2}}\,d\xi\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\geq c_{d}{\epsilon}(1-e^{-at})t^{-d/4}.

Taking
t=a^{-1}
gives the lower bound

c_{d}{\epsilon}a^{d/4}
for
0<a\leq 1
.
In dimension two this proves the asserted sharp rate
a^{1/2}
.
The initial data are fixed independently of
a
.
∎

6.
Global existence and uniform vanishing damping limit in
\mathbb{R}^{3}

In this section, we first consider global existence for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
). Since

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u\cdot\nabla)u\cdot\Delta udx\neq 0,

for
d=3
, we fail to close the global estimate for (
1.3
) in
H^{1}
. Considering the equation of vorticity
\omega
for
d=3
, one can see that there is an external high-order nonlinear term
\omega\cdot\nabla u
. By virtue of transportation structure of
\omega\cdot\nabla u
, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we fortunately close the global estimate for vorticity
\omega
through smallness condition in the critical Besov space
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
, which is not an algebra. The results of global existence play a crucial role in considering vanishing damping limit in the same topology for any
T>0
.

Then, by virtue of the improved Fourier splitting method and time weighted estimates, for any
a\in[0,1]
, we obtain optimal time decay rates and the time integrability of global solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
). Finally, we obtain the uniform vanishing damping limit and discover a new phenomenon for (
1.3
) that the sharp rate of uniform vanishing damping limit in
L^{2}
is related to the time decay rate in
L^{2}
.

6.1.
Global existence and vanishing damping limit

We first prove global existence for
a\in[0,1]
under smallness of
both the
H^{1}
norm and the critical Besov norms stated below.
As in the two-dimensional case, the estimates are uniform in
a

and include zero damping.

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
d=3
. Assume a divergence-free field
u_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}
and a symmetric matrix
\tau_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. There exists some positive constant
{\epsilon}
small enough such that if

\displaystyle\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq{\epsilon},

then (
1.3
) admits a global solution
(u,\tau)
with

(u,\tau)\in L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}).

Proof.

We start with a bootstrap assumption. Assume that for
T>0
, it holds that

(6.1)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\left(\|u\|_{H^{1}}+\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\right)\leq C_{1}{\epsilon},

where
C_{1}
is a fixed constant to be chosen later.
Firstly, we obtain the following energy identities:

(6.2)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+a\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=-\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\tau\rangle,

and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\frac{1}{2}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+a\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\langle u\cdot\nabla u,\Delta u\rangle+\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau,\Delta\tau\rangle+\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\Delta\tau\rangle.

By virtue of the estimate for the inner product, we obtain

(6.3)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\langle\eta\tau,-\nabla u\rangle+\frac{\eta}{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\eta\langle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{div}\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\mathrm{div}\tau\rangle

\displaystyle~~~+\eta\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q(\nabla u,\tau)-\Delta\tau,\nabla u\rangle.

where we choose
\eta
to be a constant such that
0\ll{\epsilon}\ll\eta\ll 1.

We deduce that

\displaystyle|\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{3}}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{1}{\epsilon}\left(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right),

and

\displaystyle\eta|\langle\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{div}\tau-u\cdot\nabla u\right),\mathrm{div}\tau\rangle|+\eta|\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q(\nabla u,\tau)-\Delta\tau,\nabla u\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\eta\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\eta\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\eta a\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle+\eta\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}+\eta\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\eta\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{4}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{a}{4}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Moreover, one can infer that

\displaystyle|\langle u\cdot\nabla u,\Delta u\rangle|+|\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau,\Delta\tau\rangle|+|\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\Delta\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle{\epsilon}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+{\epsilon}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}.

Combining these three inequalities, we deduce from (
6.2
)-(
6.3
) that

\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{2}\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{8}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{a}{2}\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq 0,

which implies that

\sup_{0\leq t<\infty}\left(\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\right)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\left(\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{4}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)dt\leq 4{\epsilon}^{2}.

To estimate
\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
, we infer that

\displaystyle\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\|\Delta_{j}\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\epsilon}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}\left\|e^{(t-s)\Delta}\Delta_{j}(u\cdot\nabla\tau+Q-Du)\right\|_{L^{\infty}}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\epsilon}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{7}{4}j}\left(\|u\cdot\nabla\tau\|_{L^{\frac{12}{7}}}+\|Q\|_{L^{\frac{12}{7}}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\epsilon}+\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{7}{4}j}\left(\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{12}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{12}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\right)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2{\epsilon}+3{\epsilon}\sum_{j\geq 0}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-(t-s)2^{2j}}2^{\frac{7}{4}j}ds\leq 30{\epsilon}.

To estimate
\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}
, we also define
\Gamma:=\omega-\mathcal{R}\tau
. Then we have

\partial_{t}\Gamma+(u\cdot\nabla)\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}\Gamma=(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau+\mathcal{R}Q+\omega\cdot\nabla u:=F.

Then by Lemma
2.9
, we get

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}t+C\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{2}(t-s)+C\int_{s}^{t}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}}\|F\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|F\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds.

By Lemmas
2.2
,
2.3
,
2.5
and
2.7
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|(a-\tfrac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim{\epsilon},

and for any
0<\delta<\frac{1}{2}
,

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\|\omega\|_{L^{2}}+\|\omega\|_{L^{\infty}})(\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{B^{\delta}_{\infty,1}})

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{1}{\epsilon}(\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

Similarly, we infer that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}Q\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|Q\|_{L^{2}}+\|Q\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{B^{\delta}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}^{2}+C_{1}\epsilon(\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}},

and

\displaystyle\|\omega\cdot\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|T_{\omega}\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|T_{\nabla u}\omega\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\rm div~\mathcal{R}(\omega,u)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\omega\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\omega\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\omega\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim{\epsilon}.

Combining these estimates, we deduce from (
6.4
) that

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}\|F\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim{\epsilon}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-s)}({\epsilon}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}})ds\lesssim{\epsilon},

which implies that

\displaystyle\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\|u\|_{L^{2}}+C\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C{\epsilon}.

In these bounds the stretching contribution is

C(C_{1}{\epsilon})^{2}
, rather than a new linear term.
Choose
C_{1}=200C
first and then choose
{\epsilon}
so small that

CC_{1}{\epsilon}\leq\eta/32
(for the energy and damped transport estimates) and

CC_{1}^{2}{\epsilon}\leq 1
.
The preceding estimates then improve (
6.1
) to

(C_{1}/2){\epsilon}
on
[0,T]
.
The critical local continuation criterion and the uniform
finite-time heat estimate extend the solution globally.
Therefore, we complete the proof of Proposition
6.1
.
∎

Now, we consider vanishing damping limit for any
T>0
under the same topology. Since the solutions
(u,\tau)
of the inviscid Oldroyd-B model (
1.3
) depend on damping
a\in[0,1]
, when considering the problems of vanishing damping limit, we denote the solutions of (
1.3
) as
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Under the conditions in Proposition
6.1
, for any
T>0
, there holds

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}\leq C_{T}a,

and

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Proof.

We record the dimension-dependent estimates needed to adapt
Proposition
5.6
. Put
U=u^{a}-u^{0}
and

\Sigma=\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}
. Integration by parts in

Q(\nabla U,\tau^{0})
and
\|\Sigma\|_{6}\lesssim\|\nabla\Sigma\|_{2}

give

(\|U\|_{2}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{2}^{2})^{\prime}+\|\nabla\Sigma\|_{2}^{2}\leq C_{T}(t)(\|U\|_{2}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{2}^{2})+Ca^{2},

where

C_{T}(t)=C(1+\|\nabla(u^{a},u^{0})\|_{\infty}+\|\tau^{0}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{3}^{2})
.
Its integral over
[0,T]
is bounded uniformly in
a
, since

\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{3}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau^{0}\|_{2}
.
Thus
\|(U,\Sigma)\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}}\leq C_{T}a
.

For later recovery of the velocity endpoint norm, the heat-block
argument in Step 1 of Proposition
5.6
gives

(6.5)

\|\Sigma\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{T}a^{1/2}.

Here use
\|U\|_{12}\lesssim\|U\|_{2}^{1/2}\|U\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}^{1/2}
and

\|\Sigma\|_{4}\leq\|\Sigma\|_{2}^{1/2}\|\Sigma\|_{\infty}^{1/2}
.
After writing
Q(\nabla U,\tau^{0})
as derivatives of
U\tau^{0}

plus
U\nabla\tau^{0}
, its latter term belongs to
L^{12/5}
.
Since
\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}_{T}L^{3}}\leq C_{T}
, Bernstein and
the
L^{4/3}
time norm of the heat kernel give

C_{T}2^{-j/4}a^{1/2}
for that term.
The divergence term
u^{a}\Sigma
gives the same bound; all
other heat-block bounds have summable factors

2^{-j/2}
or
2^{-3j/4}
. The low block is controlled by the
L^{2}
estimate.

For the strong norms take truncated solutions

(u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})
with data
(S_{N}u_{0},S_{N}\tau_{0})
.
Let
c_{N}
denote the fixed initial
H^{1}
and critical Besov tails
defined in Proposition
5.6
. On each finite interval,
the positive-index tame estimates give

(6.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\bigl(\|(u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{2}}+\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{2}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}_{T}B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}_{T}B^{3}_{\infty,1}}\bigr)\leq C_{T}2^{N}.

To see the uniformity of
C_{T}
, apply the differentiated energy
and heat-block estimates: the highest derivative in
Q
is
integrated by parts, leaving the coefficient

\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{\infty}
.
The first and third are bounded, and

\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\infty}^{2}\lesssim\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}_{T}B^{2}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{T}
.
At the positive Besov index, use

\|Q\|_{B^{1}}\lesssim\|\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{\infty}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\infty}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}}
,
absorb the transport derivative into the heat norm, and apply
Gronwall. No high-norm smallness is imposed.

Write
W_{N}=u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}
,
\Theta_{N}=\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a}
.
The lower Besov estimate (
5.41
) remains valid in dimension three:
the mixed Bony remainder is in
B^{1}_{2,1}\hookrightarrow B^{-1/2}_{\infty,1}\hookrightarrow B^{-1}_{\infty,1}
.
Its coefficient
\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{H^{2}}
has a uniform
L^{2}_{T}
bound
from Proposition
6.1
. The differentiated difference
energy and (
6.6
) also give, uniformly in
a,N
,

\|(W_{N},\Theta_{N})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}\dot{H}^{1}}+\|\nabla^{2}\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}}\leq C_{T}c_{N}.

The only potentially large transport term is

\|W_{N}\|_{2}\|\nabla^{2}u_{N}^{a}\|_{\infty}
; the factors

2^{-N}c_{N}
and
C_{T}2^{N}
cancel.

The estimates for
\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{a}
are then as in
(
5.44
), with two changes. In the
Q
term involving

\Theta_{N}
, use its
L^{2}_{T}H^{2}
bound:

\|R(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\Theta_{N})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\lesssim\|\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\Theta_{N}\|_{H^{2}}
.
For the additional stretching term use

\operatorname{div}\omega^{a}=\operatorname{div}(\omega^{a}-\omega_{N}^{a})=0

and Lemma
2.5
:

\|\omega^{a}\cdot\nabla W_{N}+(\omega^{a}-\omega_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}}\leq C{\epsilon}\bigl(\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}}+\|\Theta_{N}\|_{B^{0}}+\|(W_{N},\Theta_{N})\|_{H^{1}}\bigr).

The integrated stress endpoint error is small by
(
5.42
). Finally the coefficient

\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{H^{2}}
in the other
Q
term is absorbed using

\sup_{r}\int_{0}^{r}e^{-(r-s)/4}\|\tau_{N}^{a}(s)\|_{H^{2}}\,ds\leq C{\epsilon}
, not a pointwise high-norm bound. Hence

\|\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}}\leq C_{T}c_{N}
.

For each fixed
N
, the smooth difference energy and endpoint
transport estimates yield convergence of

(u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})
to
(u_{N}^{0},\tau_{N}^{0})
in the claimed norms,
with constants allowed to depend on
N
.
First send
a\to 0
and then
N\to\infty
.
Recover
\|\nabla U\|_{B^{0}}
from

\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma^{0}
, (
6.5
), and the low

L^{2}
block. Together with the differentiated energy estimates
this proves both assertions.
∎

6.2.
Optimal decay rates and uniform vanishing damping limit

From now on, we investigate optimal decay rate of global solutions for the inviscid Oldroyd-B equation (
1.3
) with critical regularity. Due to low regularity of the solutions, the case
a\in[0,1]
is extremely challenging. We will solve the difficulties of consistent damping and low regularity by virtue of the improved Fourier splitting method. Then we obtain optimal decay rate for the solutions in
L^{2}
. Since

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(u\cdot\nabla)u\cdot\Delta udx\neq 0,

we cannot obtain directly time decay rate of the
\dot{H}^{1}
norm for lack of external higher order regularity for the solutions. However, we find that the time integrability of global solutions for
d=3
can be obtained and helps to overcome this difficulty.

Proposition 6.3
.

Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
6.1
, if additionally
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in\dot{B}^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{2,\infty},

then there holds

\|(u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+(1+t)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}

and

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\tau,\nabla u)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\leq C_{0},

where
C_{0}
depends on
\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}\cap\dot{B}^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{2,\infty}}
and
\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

Proof.

The proof of Proposition
6.3
relies on a series of
iteration technique
. For sake of clarity, we first present the main ideas in the following chart.

\displaystyle\text{Energy and Fourier splitting}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-1/8},\ (1+t)^{-1/4},\ (1+t)^{-3/8},

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-3/8}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\sup_{t}\|(u,\tau)\|_{\dot{B}^{-3/2}_{2,\infty}}<\infty\longrightarrow\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-3/4},

\displaystyle\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-3/4}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\text{key integrability}\longrightarrow\|\nabla(u,\tau)\|_{2}\lesssim(1+t)^{-5/4}.

We divide the proof into the following five steps.

Step 1: Initial decay rate.

According to Proposition
6.1
, for any
t>0
, we have

(6.7)

\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+2\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle\right)+\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{\eta}{4}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+a\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\leq 0.

Define
S(t)=\{\xi:|\xi|^{2}\leq C_{1}(1+t)^{-1}\}
with
C_{1}
large enough. Considering the issue of time decay rates, we can assume that
t
is sufficiently large relative to
C_{1}
. Applying Schonbek’s
[
39
]
strategy to (
6.7
), we have

\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}}=\int_{S(t)}(1+|\xi|^{2})|\xi|^{2}|\hat{\tau}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi+\int_{S(t)^{c}}(1+|\xi|^{2})|\xi|^{2}|\hat{\tau}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi.

and

\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}=\int_{S(t)}|\xi|^{2}|\hat{u}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi+\int_{S(t)^{c}}|\xi|^{2}|\hat{u}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi.

One can easily deduce that

\frac{C_{1}}{1+t}\int_{S(t)^{c}}(1+|\xi|^{2})|\hat{\tau}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi\leq\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}},

and

\frac{C_{1}}{1+t}\int_{S(t)^{c}}|\hat{u}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi\leq\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}.

By (
6.7
), we obtain

(6.8)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+2\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{\eta C_{1}}{8(1+t)}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\leq\frac{C}{1+t}\int_{S(t)}|\hat{u}(\xi)|^{2}+|\hat{\tau}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi.

The
L^{2}
estimate to the low frequency of
(u,\tau)
is the key to studying time decay rates. Applying Fourier transform to (
1.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\hat{u}^{j}_{t}+i\xi_{j}\hat{P}-i\xi_{k}\hat{\tau}^{jk}=\hat{F}^{j},\\[4.30554pt]
\hat{\tau}^{jk}_{t}+|\xi|^{2}\hat{\tau}^{jk}+a\hat{\tau}^{jk}-\frac{i}{2}(\xi_{k}\hat{u}^{j}+\xi_{j}\hat{u}^{k})=\hat{G}^{jk},\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

where
F=-u\cdot\nabla u
and
G=-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q(\nabla u,\tau)
.
According to (
6.2
), we get

(6.11)

\displaystyle\int_{S(t)}|\hat{u}|^{2}+|\hat{\tau}|^{2}d\xi\lesssim\int_{S(t)}|\hat{u}_{0}|^{2}+|\hat{\tau}_{0}|^{2}d\xi+{\underbrace{\int_{S(t)}\int_{0}^{t}|\hat{F}\cdot\overline{\hat{u}}|+|\hat{G}\cdot\overline{\hat{\tau}}|dt^{\prime}d\xi}_{\coloneqq\mathcal{N}(u,\tau)}}.

According to
(u_{0},\tau_{0})\in L^{2}\cap\dot{B}^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{2,\infty}
and applying Lemma
2.1
, we have

(6.12)

\displaystyle\int_{S(t)}|\hat{u}_{0}|^{2}+|\hat{\tau}_{0}|^{2}d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\leq\log_{2}[\frac{4}{3}C_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}]}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}2\varphi^{2}(2^{-j}\xi)(|\hat{u}_{0}|^{2}+|\hat{\tau}_{0}|^{2})d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\leq\log_{2}[\frac{4}{3}C_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}]}2(\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}u_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau_{0}\|^{2}_{L^{2}})

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|^{2}_{\dot{B}^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{2,\infty}}.

Moreover, we infer from (
6.7
) that

(6.13)

\displaystyle\int_{S(t)}\int_{0}^{t}|\hat{F}\cdot\overline{\hat{u}}|+|\hat{G}\cdot\overline{\hat{\tau}}|dt^{\prime}d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\int_{0}^{t}\|\hat{F}\cdot\overline{\hat{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}+\|\hat{G}\cdot\overline{\hat{\tau}}\|_{L^{2}_{\xi}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\int_{0}^{t}(\|u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}})(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Combining (
6.8
)-(
6.13
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+2\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{\eta C_{1}}{8(1+t)}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{4}}

which implies that

(6.14)

\displaystyle\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{4}}.

Plugging (
6.14
) into (
6.13
), we obtain

(6.15)

\displaystyle\int_{S(t)}\int_{0}^{t}|\hat{F}\cdot\overline{\hat{u}}|+|\hat{G}\cdot\overline{\hat{\tau}}|dt^{\prime}d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{4}}(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Combining (
6.8
)-(
6.12
) and (
6.15
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+2\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{\eta C_{1}}{8(1+t)}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}

which implies that

(6.16)

\displaystyle\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

This together with (
6.7
) ensure that

(6.17)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}\frac{1}{\delta},

where
\delta\in(0,\frac{1}{2})
.
Plugging (
6.16
) into (
6.13
), we infer from (
6.17
) that

(6.18)

\displaystyle\int_{S(t)}\int_{0}^{t}|\hat{F}\cdot\overline{\hat{u}}|+|\hat{G}\cdot\overline{\hat{\tau}}|dt^{\prime}d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}.

Combining (
6.8
)-(
6.12
) and (
6.18
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+2\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{\eta C_{1}}{8(1+t)}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{7}{4}}

which implies that

(6.19)

\displaystyle\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}.

This together with (
6.7
) ensure that

(6.20)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{\frac{3}{4}-\delta}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}\frac{1}{\delta},

where
\delta\in(0,\frac{3}{4})
.

Step 2: Uniform bounds in negative Besov space.

Applying
\dot{\Delta}_{j}
to (
1.3
), we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\dot{\Delta}_{j}u_{t}+\nabla\dot{\Delta}_{j}P-{\rm div}~\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau=\dot{\Delta}_{j}F,\\[4.30554pt]
\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau_{t}-\Delta\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau+a\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau-\dot{\Delta}_{j}D(u)=\dot{\Delta}_{j}G.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

We firstly infer from (
6.2
) that

(6.23)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}(\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}})+2\|\nabla\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}F\|_{L^{2}}\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}G\|_{L^{2}}\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

Set

H(t)=\sup_{0\leq r\leq t}\left(\|u(r)\|_{\dot{B}^{-3/2}_{2,\infty}}+\|\tau(r)\|_{\dot{B}^{-3/2}_{2,\infty}}\right).

Multiplying (
6.23
) by
2^{-3j}
and integrating before
taking the supremum gives

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq r\leq t}\sup_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{-3j}\left(\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}u(r)\|_{2}^{2}+\|\dot{\Delta}_{j}\tau(r)\|_{2}^{2}\right)

(6.24)

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim H^{2}(0)+H(t)\int_{0}^{t}\left(\|F(s)\|_{\dot{B}^{-3/2}_{2,\infty}}+\|G(s)\|_{\dot{B}^{-3/2}_{2,\infty}}\right)\,ds.

The paired-block supremum on the left is equivalent to
H^{2}(t)
.
One may first restrict the dyadic supremum to a finite index range;
the bounds below are independent of that range, so passage to the limit
also justifies finiteness of
H(t)
. Consequently,

(6.25)

\displaystyle H^{2}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim H^{2}(0)+H(t)\int_{0}^{t}\|F\|_{\dot{B}^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{2,\infty}}+\|G\|_{\dot{B}^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{2,\infty}}dt^{\prime}.

Using (
6.19
), (
6.20
) and inclusion between Lesbesgue and Besov space, we infer that

(6.26)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|(F,G)\|_{\dot{B}^{-\frac{3}{2}}_{2,\infty}}ds^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|(F,G)\|_{L^{1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}(\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}})(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{-\frac{3}{8}}(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}.

Combining (
6.25
) and (
6.26
), for any
t>0
, we obtain

\displaystyle H(t)\lesssim C_{0}.

Step 3: Optimal decay rate in
L^{2}
.

According to (
6.8
)-(
6.12
), we infer that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+2\eta\langle\tau,-\nabla u\rangle\right)+\frac{\eta C_{1}}{8(1+t)}\|(u,\tau)\|^{2}_{H^{1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+t}\int_{S(t)}|\hat{u}(\xi)|^{2}+|\hat{\tau}(\xi)|^{2}d\xi

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle H^{2}(t)(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}}\lesssim C_{0}^{2}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}}.

As a result, we conclude that

\displaystyle\left\|(u,\tau)\right\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{2}}.

This together with (
6.7
) ensure that

(6.27)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{\frac{5}{2}}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}})dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t),

and

(6.28)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{\frac{3}{2}-\delta}(\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}})dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}\frac{1}{\delta},

where
\delta\in(0,\frac{3}{2})
.

Step 4: The key integrability.

However, considering the decay rate for the first derivative of the solution to (
1.3
), the main difficulty is the lack of control of the higher order energy. To overcome this difficulty, we introduce a new method which flexibly combines the time weighted energy estimate and the following key integrability:

\int_{0}^{\infty}\|(\tau,\nabla u)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\leq C_{0}.

According to (
6.28
), for any
t>0
, we infer that

(6.29)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta_{-1}\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\Delta_{-1}\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{L^{6}}dt^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(Id-\Delta_{-1})\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{\frac{5}{4}}\|\nabla\tau\|^{2}_{H^{1}}dt^{\prime}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim C_{0},

Notice that
\int_{0}^{t}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}
can be derived by the same method. Recall that

\partial_{t}\Gamma+(u\cdot\nabla)\Gamma+\frac{1}{2}\Gamma=(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau+\mathcal{R}Q+\omega\cdot\nabla u.

where
\Gamma=\omega-\mathcal{R}\tau
.
Then by Proposition
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}e^{-\frac{1}{4}t}+\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{1}{4}(t-t^{\prime})}\|(a-\frac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau+\mathcal{R}Q+\omega\cdot\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}.

This together with (
6.29
), Lemmas
2.8
,
2.5
and
2.7
ensures that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|(a-\tfrac{1}{2})\mathcal{R}\tau+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau+\mathcal{R}Q+\omega\cdot\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+(\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\omega\|_{L^{2}})(\|\tau\|_{B^{\frac{1}{4}}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}})dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle~~~+\int_{0}^{t}\|\omega\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}dt^{\prime}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\omega\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}+{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{t}\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}.

Then we have

(6.30)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\mathcal{R}\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim C_{0}.

Step 5: Optimal decay rate in
\dot{H}^{1}
.

According to (
1.3
), we have

(6.31)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+a\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}=\langle u\cdot\nabla u,\Delta u\rangle+\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau,\Delta\tau\rangle+\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\Delta\tau\rangle.

Then we have

\displaystyle|\langle u\cdot\nabla u,\Delta u\rangle|+|\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau,\Delta\tau\rangle|+|\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\Delta\tau\rangle|

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{6}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|u\|_{L^{3}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|^{2}_{L^{2}}+C\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}.

This together with (
6.31
) implies that

(6.32)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}.

Multiplying
(1+t)^{\frac{7}{2}}
to (
6.32
), then we get

(6.33)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left[(1+t)^{\frac{7}{2}}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right]\lesssim(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})(1+t)^{\frac{7}{2}}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+(1+t)^{\frac{5}{2}}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Integrating
t
at both sides of (
6.33
), applying Gronwall’s inequality and using (
6.27
), (
6.30
), we obtain

\displaystyle(1+t)^{\frac{7}{2}}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim e^{\int_{0}^{t}(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tau\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}})\,dt^{\prime}}\left(\left\|\nabla(u_{0},\tau_{0})\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}(1+t^{\prime})^{\frac{5}{2}}\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}(1+t),

which implies that

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(u,\tau)\right\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim C_{0}(1+t)^{-\frac{5}{2}}.

Thus we complete the proof of Proposition
6.3
.
∎

By virtue of the optimal decay rates and key integrability in Proposition
6.3
, we finally obtain the uniform vanishing damping limit and prove that the rate of uniform vanishing damping limit in
L^{2}
is related to the time decay rate in
L^{2}
.

Proposition 6.4
.

Under the conditions in Proposition
6.3
, there holds

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2})}\leq Ca^{\frac{3}{4}},

and

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,\infty;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

Proof.

By direct calculation, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|^{2}_{L^{2}}(\|\nabla(u^{0},u^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tau^{0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|^{2}_{L^{3}})

\displaystyle+a\|\tau^{a}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}.

Then, we deduce that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle a(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}+\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\left(\|\nabla(u^{0},u^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tau^{0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|^{2}_{L^{3}}\right).

We infer from Propositions
6.1
and
6.3
that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\|\nabla(u^{0},u^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tau^{0}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|^{2}_{L^{3}}dt\lesssim 1.

Using Gronwall’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim a(1+t)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

If
at\leq 1
, we get

\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

If
at>1
, by Proposition
6.3
, we obtain

\|(u^{a}-u^{0},\tau^{a}-\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})\|_{L^{2}}+\|(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim(1+t)^{-\frac{3}{4}}\leq a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

For the global high-order convergence, thanks to Propositions
6.2
and
6.3
, it is the same as the proof of Proposition
5.8
. Hence we finish the proof of Proposition
6.4
.
∎

In the following we show that the vanishing damping limit in Proposition
6.4
is sharp in
a
in the following sense.

Proposition 6.5
.

There exists initial data
(u_{0},\tau_{0})
satisfying the conditions of Proposition
6.4
such that the following estimates hold:

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,\infty;L^{2})}\gtrsim a^{\frac{3}{4}}.

Proof.

Use the exact isotropic solution (
5.62
)
with
d=3
. The same fixed Schwartz initial stress lies in

\dot{B}^{-3/2}_{2,\infty}
; at
t=a^{-1}
the computation in
Proposition
5.9
gives

\|\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}\|_{2}\geq c_{3}{\epsilon}a^{3/4}
.
∎

The proof of Theorem
1.2
:

Combining Propositions
5.3
,
5.6
,
6.1
and
6.2
, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

\Box

The proof of Theorem
1.7
:

Combining Propositions
5.7
,
5.8
,
6.3
,
6.4
,
5.9
and
6.5
, we finish the proof of Theorem
1.7
.

\Box

7.
Dynamics in the periodic domains
\mathbb{T}^{d}

In this section we consider Oldroyd-B models in the periodic domain

\mathbb{T}^{d}=(\mathbb{R}/2\pi\mathbb{Z})^{d}
for
d=2,3
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\partial_{t}u+u\cdot\nabla u+\nabla P={\rm div}~\tau,~~~~{\rm div}~u=0,\\[4.30554pt]
\partial_{t}\tau+u\cdot\nabla\tau+a\tau+Q(\nabla u,\tau)=2D(u)+\Delta\tau,\\[4.30554pt]
u|_{t=0}=u_{0},~~\tau|_{t=0}=\tau_{0}.\\[4.30554pt]
\end{array}\right.

Here we choose the coupling coefficient
\alpha=2
in order to agree with the
normalization used in the numerical models below. The same arguments apply to
the normalization
\alpha=1
in (
1.3
); in that case the coefficient of the
good unknown
\Gamma
below is
1/2
instead of
1
. We use the periodic
inhomogeneous Littlewood–Paley decomposition throughout this
section and write
B^{s}_{p,r}=B^{s}_{p,r}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
for simplicity. The product,
commutator and transport estimates stated in the preliminary section remain valid
on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
. The zero Fourier mode, which is contained in the lowest dyadic block,
will be treated separately below. Exponential decay on the torus has also been
obtained for related incompressible Oldroyd-B systems in an analytic framework;
see, for example,
[
13
]
. Here we record the corresponding relaxation
mechanism for the present Sobolev–Besov setting.

7.1.
Well-posedness and vanishing damping limit results in the periodic case

The spatial average of the velocity is conserved. Moreover, the system is
Galilean invariant: if
c=\overline{u_{0}}
, then

(7.4)

\widetilde{u}(t,x)=u(t,x+ct)-c,\qquad\widetilde{\tau}(t,x)=\tau(t,x+ct),\qquad\widetilde{P}(t,x)=P(t,x+ct)

solves the same system and satisfies
\overline{\widetilde{u}_{0}}=0
. We shall
therefore work, without loss of generality, in the zero-momentum frame

(7.5)

\overline{u_{0}}=0.

For data with nonzero velocity average, all the conclusions below follow
after (
7.4
), with
u
replaced by

u-\overline{u_{0}}
and with
\mathcal{E}_{0}
computed from

(u_{0}-\overline{u_{0}},\tau_{0})
. No analogous zero-mean normalization is imposed on

\tau
: its spatial average obeys a forced ODE, damped when
a>0
, with
forcing generated by
Q(\nabla u,\tau)
, and therefore is not conserved.

Our main results for
\mathbb{T}^{d}
with
d=2,3
can be stated as follows.

Theorem 7.1

(
Local well-posedness).

Let
d=2,3
and
a\in[0,1]
. Assume that
u_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}
is divergence free with
\overline{u_{0}}=0
, and that

\tau_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}
is symmetric. Set

\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

There exist a lifespan
T=T(\mathcal{E}_{0})>0
, such that (
7
)
admits a unique local solution
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
satisfying

\displaystyle u^{a}\in C([0,T];H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}),

\displaystyle\tau^{a}\in C([0,T];H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}(0,T;B^{2}_{\infty,1}),

and

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big(\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big)

(7.6)

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{T}\|\tau^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}dt+\left(\int_{0}^{T}\|\nabla\tau^{a}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Moreover, there exists a constant
C_{T}
, independent
of
a
, such that

(7.7)

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}\leq C_{T}a.

Theorem 7.2

(
Global well-posedness).

Let
d=2,3
and
a\in[0,1]
. Assume that
u_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}
is divergence free with
\overline{u_{0}}=0
, and that

\tau_{0}\in H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}
is symmetric. Set

\mathcal{E}_{0}:=\|(u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{H^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}+\|(\nabla u_{0},\tau_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}.

There exist constants
{\epsilon}_{0}>0
and
C>0
, independent of

a\in[0,1]
, such that if
\mathcal{E}_{0}\leq{\epsilon}_{0}
, then (
7
)
admits a unique global solution
(u^{a},\tau^{a})
satisfying

\displaystyle u^{a}\in C([0,\infty);H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}),

\displaystyle\tau^{a}\in C([0,\infty);H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}_{\rm loc}(0,\infty;B^{2}_{\infty,1}),

and

\displaystyle\sup_{t\geq 0}\big(\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u^{a},\tau^{a})(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big)

(7.8)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\big(\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau^{a}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+a\|\tau^{a}\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)\,dt\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Moreover, for every
T>0
, there exists a constant
C_{T}
, independent
of
a
, such that

(7.9)

\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;L^{2})}\leq C_{T}a,

and

(7.10)

\lim_{a\rightarrow 0}\|(u^{a},\tau^{a})-(u^{0},\tau^{0})\|_{L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}(0,T;\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}=0.

We only present the details of the proof of Theorem
7.2
since the proof of Theorem
7.1
follows from the same fashion.

Proof of Theorem
7.2
.

We divide the proof into four steps. All constants below are uniform for

a\in[0,1]
.

Step 1. Local theory and the continuation criterion.

Let
(\Delta_{j})_{j\geq-1}
be the inhomogeneous periodic dyadic blocks. The
periodic versions of Lemmas
2.4
,
2.5
,
2.6
,
2.8
,

2.9
,
3.1
and Corollary
4.3
follow either by transference or
directly by summing over
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
. In particular, their constants
do not depend on
a
. We normalize the pressure by
\overline{P}=0
; all
inverse Laplacians below act on nonzero Fourier modes.
Applying the Fourier truncation scheme of Proposition
3.3
, with

D(u)
there replaced by
2D(u)
, produces a solution on
[0,T_{0}]
, where

T_{0}
depends only on the initial norm appearing in the theorem, and

\displaystyle u

\displaystyle\in C([0,T_{0}];H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}),

\displaystyle\tau

\displaystyle\in C([0,T_{0}];H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})\cap L^{1}(0,T_{0};B^{2}_{\infty,1}).

The same transport and parabolic estimates applied to the difference of two
approximations give convergence, uniqueness and continuous dependence. The
corresponding blow-up alternative says that the solution can be continued
past
T<\infty
provided

(7.11)

\sup_{0\leq t<T}\big(\|(u,\tau)(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u,\tau)(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big)<\infty.

Indeed, on a finite interval the bound in (
7.11
) inserted
in the periodic parabolic estimate gives

\tau\in L^{1}(0,T;B^{2}_{\infty,1})
, so the local construction can be
restarted with a lifespan depending only on the displayed bound.
This also gives continuous dependence on the initial data on every interval
on which the quantity in (
7.11
) is bounded.

Step 2. Uniform
H^{1}
estimate and the zero Fourier mode.

For a periodic function
f
, write

\overline{f}=\frac{1}{|\mathbb{T}^{d}|}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}f(x)\,dx,\qquad m=\overline{\tau},\qquad\sigma=\tau-m.

Taking spatial averages in (
7
) gives

(7.12)

\overline{u}(t)=\overline{u_{0}}=0,\qquad m^{\prime}(t)+am(t)=-\overline{Q(\nabla u,\sigma)}.

Indeed,
\overline{D(u)}=\overline{\Omega(u)}=0
, and hence

\overline{Q(\nabla u,m)}=0
. In particular,
u
and
\sigma
have zero
mean, while
\nabla\sigma=\nabla\tau
.
The velocity normalization now permits the direct Poincaré estimate

(7.13)

\|u\|_{H^{1}}\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}.

The stress requires a separate treatment because only
\sigma
, rather than

\tau
, has zero mean.

Fix a small number
\delta>0
and make the bootstrap assumption

(7.14)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big(\|(u,\tau)(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\|(\nabla u,\tau)(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big)\leq\delta.

Testing the velocity equation by
u
and the stress equation by

\frac{1}{2}\tau
cancels the linear coupling terms. The same cancellation holds
after testing by
-\Delta u
and
-\frac{1}{2}\Delta\tau
, respectively. More precisely, if

\mathscr{E}_{\rm b}=\frac{1}{2}\|u\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2},

then the two coupling terms cancel at both derivative levels and

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathscr{E}_{\rm b}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{a}{2}\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\tau\rangle+\langle u\cdot\nabla u,\Delta u\rangle

(7.15)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\langle u\cdot\nabla\tau,\Delta\tau\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\Delta\tau\rangle.

Since
\sigma
has zero mean, Poincaré’s
inequality and
d\leq 3
give

(7.16)

\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}}+\|\sigma\|_{L^{4}}\leq C\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L^{2}}.

The part of
Q(\nabla u,m)
paired with
m
has zero integral. Expanding all
other terms and using (
7.16
), we find

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}Q(\nabla u,m+\sigma):(m+\sigma)\,dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\big(|m|+\|\sigma\|_{H^{1}}\big)\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L^{2}}

(7.17)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big).

At the differentiated level we use, without differentiating
Q
,

(7.18)

|\langle Q(\nabla u,\tau),\Delta\tau\rangle|\leq C\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\Delta\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

The differentiated transport terms are bounded by

C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2})
.
Here, in dimension two, one may also use

\langle u\cdot\nabla u,\Delta u\rangle=0
; in dimension three its absolute
value is at most

C\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
.
Equations (
7.17
)–(
7.18
), the transport
estimate above, and the bootstrap assumption therefore imply

(7.19)

\left|\text{the right-hand side of
\eqref{7per-basic-H1-identity}}\right|\leq C\delta\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big).

To recover the velocity dissipation, choose
0<\eta\ll 1
and introduce

\mathscr{E}_{1}(t)=\frac{1}{2}\|u(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\tau(t)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}-\eta\langle\sigma(t),\nabla u(t)\rangle.

Since
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}m:\nabla u\,dx=0
, the mixed term is also

-\eta\langle\tau,\nabla u\rangle
. Let
\mathbb{P}
be the periodic Leray
projector, with its zero mode fixed in the usual way. Since

u_{t}=\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{div}\tau-u\cdot\nabla u)
, direct
differentiation gives the exact identity

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\big(-\eta\langle\tau,\nabla u\rangle\big)+\eta\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\eta\langle\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{div}\sigma-u\cdot\nabla u),\operatorname{div}\sigma\rangle

(7.20)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\eta\langle u\cdot\nabla\sigma+a\sigma+Q(\nabla u,m+\sigma)-\Delta\sigma,\nabla u\rangle.

By
(
7.14
), Poincaré’s inequality and
a\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\eta\left|\langle\mathbb{P}(\operatorname{div}\sigma-u\cdot\nabla u),\operatorname{div}\sigma\rangle\right|

(7.21)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\eta\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\eta\delta\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big),

\displaystyle\eta\left|\langle u\cdot\nabla\sigma+a\sigma+Q(\nabla u,m+\sigma)-\Delta\sigma,\nabla u\rangle\right|

(7.22)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\eta}{4}\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\eta\|\nabla\sigma\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\frac{a}{8}\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C(\eta\delta+\eta^{2}a)\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Here we used
|m|\leq\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}
and
\|u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\delta
, which follows from
(
7.14
), the velocity mean bound, and

B^{0}_{\infty,1}\hookrightarrow L^{\infty}
. Combining
(
7.15
)–(
7.22
), and then choosing
first
\eta
and next
\delta
small, we obtain

(7.23)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mathscr{E}_{1}(t)+c\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+a\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)\leq 0.

Moreover,
\mathscr{E}_{1}\simeq\|(u,\tau)\|_{H^{1}}^{2}
. Then we have

(7.24)

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|(u,\tau)(t)\|_{H^{1}}+\left(\int_{0}^{T}\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+a\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\big)dt\right)^{1/2}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Step 3. Uniform critical estimate and global continuation.

We first make the periodic heat estimate explicit. The lowest dyadic block
contains only finitely many Fourier modes, so

(7.25)

\|\Delta_{-1}\tau(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\|\tau(t)\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

For
j\geq 0
, Duhamel’s formula and the spectral gap on the support of

\Delta_{j}
give

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}\tau(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-c2^{2j}t}\|\Delta_{j}\tau_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}

(7.26)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c2^{2j}(t-s)}\|\Delta_{j}(2D(u)-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q(\nabla u,\tau))(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\,ds.

In two dimensions, use
H^{1}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\hookrightarrow L^{8}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
and Bernstein’s
inequality yields

(7.27)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}(2D(u)-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q)(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C2^{\frac{3}{2}j}\big(

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{8}}\|\tau\|_{L^{8}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{4}}\big)(s).

In three dimensions, Hölder’s and Bernstein’s inequalities with

p=12/7
instead give

(7.28)

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}(2D(u)-u\cdot\nabla\tau-Q)(s)\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C2^{\frac{7}{4}j}\big(

\displaystyle\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}+\|u\|_{L^{12}}\|\nabla\tau\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{12}}\big)(s).

Under (
7.14
), the quantities in parentheses in
(
7.27
) and (
7.28
) are bounded by

C\mathcal{E}_{0}
. Since

\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c2^{2j}(t-s)}ds\leq C2^{-2j},

the high-frequency remainders are bounded respectively by

C2^{-j/2}\mathcal{E}_{0}
and
C2^{-j/4}\mathcal{E}_{0}
. Both sequences are
summable. Together with (
7.25
), this proves

(7.29)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\tau(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Define on periodic distributions

\mathcal{R}=-(-\Delta)^{-1}{\rm curl}\,{\rm div},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{R}f}(0)=0,\qquad\Gamma=\omega-\mathcal{R}\tau.

For every nonzero Fourier mode,
\mathcal{R}(2D(u))=\omega
. In dimension
two,
\Gamma
satisfies

(7.30)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma+u\cdot\nabla\Gamma+\Gamma=(a-1)\mathcal{R}\tau+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u,\tau)+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau.

In dimension three, it satisfies instead

(7.31)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\Gamma+u\cdot\nabla\Gamma+\Gamma=(a-1)\mathcal{R}\tau+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u,\tau)+[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau+\omega\cdot\nabla u.

Thus there is no dimension-dependent indicator hidden in the notation. The
coefficient of
\Gamma
is
1
because the stress equation contains

2D(u)
.

The periodic multiplier and commutator bounds take the form

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}f\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|f\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|f\|_{L^{2}}\big),

(7.32)

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}}\big)\big(\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau\|_{L^{2}}\big).

We spell out the nonlinear estimates needed here. Fix

0<\varepsilon<1
if
d=2
and
0<\varepsilon<1/2
if
d=3
. Periodic
Sobolev embedding and the dyadic product law give

(7.33)

\displaystyle\|\tau\|_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\infty,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|\tau\|_{H^{1}}+\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}\big),

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{L^{2}}+\|Q(\nabla u,\tau)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\|\nabla u\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau\|_{B^{\varepsilon}_{\infty,\infty}}

(7.34)

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}^{2}+C\delta\mathcal{E}_{0}+C\delta\|\nabla^{2}\tau\|_{L^{2}}.

The last inequality uses (
7.24
),
(
7.29
) and the bootstrap bound. It includes the contribution

Q(\nabla u,m)
: although
\mathcal{R}m=0
, this product need not vanish, and
it is controlled by
|m|\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
in
(
7.34
). Likewise, (
7.32
) yields

(7.35)

\|[\mathcal{R},u\cdot\nabla]\tau\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(\delta+\mathcal{E}_{0})\mathcal{E}_{0}.

In dimension three we use
\operatorname{div}\omega=0
to write

\omega\cdot\nabla u=\operatorname{div}(\omega\otimes u)
. The periodic
Bony decomposition, with the derivative placed on the
B^{1}_{\infty,1}

factor, gives

\displaystyle\|\omega\cdot\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\omega\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

(7.36)

\displaystyle\leq C\delta\|\Gamma\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\delta\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Here we used
\omega=\Gamma+\mathcal{R}\tau
and

\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})
.
Therefore, if
F_{2}
and
F_{3}
denote the complete right-hand sides of
(
7.30
) and (
7.31
), respectively, then

\displaystyle\|F_{2}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}+C\delta\|\nabla^{2}\tau(t)\|_{L^{2}},

(7.37)

\displaystyle\|F_{3}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}+C\delta\|\nabla^{2}\tau(t)\|_{L^{2}}+C\delta\|\Gamma(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

The last term is present only in dimension three. The damped transport
estimate, with
\delta
small
enough that
C\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq 1/4
, gives

(7.38)

\|\Gamma(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq Ce^{-ct}\|\Gamma_{0}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+C\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}\|F_{d}(s)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\,ds.

By Cauchy–Schwarz and (
7.24
),

\sup_{t\leq T}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-c(t-s)}\|\nabla^{2}\tau(s)\|_{L^{2}}\,ds\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Consequently, after absorbing the last term of
(
7.37
) in dimension three, we obtain

(7.39)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\Gamma(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Finally, the zero mode of
\nabla u
vanishes, and the periodic Biot–Savart
estimate, (
7.29
) and (
7.39
) imply

(7.40)

\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\nabla u(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\big(\|\Gamma(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big)\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Together with

\|u\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C(\|u\|_{L^{2}}+\|\nabla u\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}})
, this also gives the full

B^{1}_{\infty,1}
bound for the velocity.
Choose
\mathcal{E}_{0}
so small that
C\mathcal{E}_{0}\leq\delta/2
.
Equations (
7.24
), (
7.29
) and
(
7.40
) improve the bootstrap assumption. A standard
continuity argument and (
7.11
) therefore give the unique
global solution and (
7.8
). Applying the periodic
parabolic estimate to the stress equation on each finite interval also gives

\tau\in L^{1}_{\rm loc}(0,\infty;B^{2}_{\infty,1})
.

Step 4. Vanishing damping limit.

Put
U=u^{a}-u^{0}
,
\Sigma=\tau^{a}-\tau^{0}
and

\Pi=P^{a}-P^{0}
. The difference satisfies

(7.41)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\partial_{t}U+u^{a}\cdot\nabla U+U\cdot\nabla u^{0}+\nabla\Pi=\operatorname{div}\Sigma,\qquad\operatorname{div}U=0,\\
&\partial_{t}\Sigma+u^{a}\cdot\nabla\Sigma+U\cdot\nabla\tau^{0}+a\tau^{a}+Q(\nabla u^{a},\Sigma)+Q(\nabla U,\tau^{0})\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt=2D(U)+\Delta\Sigma.\end{aligned}\right.

The terms
Q(\nabla U,\tau^{0})
and

U\cdot\nabla\tau^{0}
require some care because the velocity difference has
no direct diffusion. Using the tensor structure of
Q
and integrating the
derivative of
U
by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle|\langle Q(\nabla U,\tau^{0}),\Sigma\rangle|+|\langle U\cdot\nabla\tau^{0},\Sigma\rangle|

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\|U\|_{L^{2}}\big(\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{r_{d}}}\|\Sigma\|_{L^{q_{d}}}+\|\tau^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}\big)

(7.42)

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{8}\|\nabla\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+CA_{d}(t)\big(\|U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big),

where

(r_{2},q_{2},\rho_{2})=(4,4,4/3),\qquad(r_{3},q_{3},\rho_{3})=(3,6,2),

and

A_{d}(t)=1+\|\nabla u^{a}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{0}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\tau^{0}(t)\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+\|\nabla\tau^{0}(t)\|_{L^{r_{d}}}^{\rho_{d}}.

The Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequalities and
(
7.8
) show that

(7.43)

\int_{0}^{T}A_{d}(t)\,dt\leq C_{T}.

For example,

\displaystyle\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{4}}^{4/3}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2/3}\|\nabla^{2}\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}^{2/3}\qquad

\displaystyle(d=2),

\displaystyle\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{3}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla\tau^{0}\|_{L^{6}}\qquad

\displaystyle(d=3).

The remaining nonlinear terms in (
7.41
) satisfy

\displaystyle|\langle U\cdot\nabla u^{0},U\rangle|+|\langle Q(\nabla u^{a},\Sigma),\Sigma\rangle|\leq C\big(\|\nabla u^{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big)\big(\|U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big).

Testing the first equation in (
7.41
) by
U
and the
second one by
\frac{1}{2}\Sigma
cancels the two linear coupling terms.
Together with

a|\langle\tau^{a},\Sigma\rangle|\leq Ca^{2}\|\tau^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}
, this yields

(7.44)

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left(\frac{1}{2}\|U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{4}\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq CA_{d}(t)\big(\|U\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Sigma\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big)+Ca^{2}\|\tau^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

Since
(U,\Sigma)|_{t=0}=0
, (
7.43
), the
uniform
L^{2}
bound and Gronwall’s inequality prove
(
7.9
).

We finally give the Bona–Smith argument at the endpoint. Let
S_{N}
be the
periodic Fourier cut-off to
|k|\lesssim 2^{N}
, let

z^{a}=(u^{a},\tau^{a})
, and let
z_{N}^{a}=(u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})
be the solution with
initial data
(S_{N}u_{0},S_{N}\tau_{0})
. Set

W_{N}^{a}=u^{a}-u_{N}^{a},\qquad\Theta_{N}^{a}=\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a},

and

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{N}={}

\displaystyle\|(I-S_{N})u_{0}\|_{H^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|(I-S_{N})\tau_{0}\|_{H^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

Then
\varepsilon_{N}\to 0
. The high-frequency localization of the initial
tails gives

(7.45)

\|(W_{N}^{a},\Theta_{N}^{a})(0)\|_{L^{2}}+\|W_{N}^{a}(0)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}(0)\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C2^{-N}\varepsilon_{N}.

The
L^{2}
difference estimate used above, now with the nonzero initial
difference in (
7.45
), gives

(7.46)

\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\left(\|(W_{N}^{a},\Theta_{N}^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{2}}+\|\nabla\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}}\right)\leq C_{T}2^{-N}\varepsilon_{N}.

We next prove, rather than assume, the stability estimate one Besov index
below the endpoint. We begin with the uniform maximal-regularity bound
needed in that argument. Write

\partial_{t}\tau_{N}^{a}-\Delta\tau_{N}^{a}+a\tau_{N}^{a}=F_{N}^{a},\qquad F_{N}^{a}=2D(u_{N}^{a})-u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}-Q(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a}).

Let
I=[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset[0,T]
. For every
j\geq 0
, the blockwise heat
estimate, with
e^{-a(t-s)}\leq 1
, gives

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{j}\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(I;L^{\infty})}+2^{2j}\|\Delta_{j}\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;L^{\infty})}

(7.47)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\left(\|\Delta_{j}\tau_{N}^{a}(t_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Delta_{j}F_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;L^{\infty})}\right),

where
C
is independent of
a
and
N
. The zero Fourier mode contained
in
\Delta_{-1}
has no heat smoothing. Since its
B^{2}_{\infty,1}
weight
is only the fixed number
2^{-2}
, it is instead estimated directly by

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{-1}\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(I;L^{\infty})}+2^{-2}\|\Delta_{-1}\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;L^{\infty})}

(7.48)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C(1+|I|)\left(\|\Delta_{-1}\tau_{N}^{a}(t_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|\Delta_{-1}F_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;L^{\infty})}\right).

Thus the zero mode changes only the harmless factor
1+|I|
and introduces
no factor depending on
a^{-1}
.

Set

X_{I}=\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}(I;B^{0}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{2}_{\infty,1})}.

The transport structure in Lemma
2.5
and the product estimate there,
used with positive exponent
1/2
, imply

(7.49)

\displaystyle\|F_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+C\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\big(\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1/2}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\big).

Here
u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}=\operatorname{div}(u_{N}^{a}\otimes\tau_{N}^{a})
is estimated by the
transport inequality, rather than by treating two
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
factors
as an algebra. The two intermediate stress norms are controlled on
I
by
the explicit interpolation inequalities

(7.50)

\displaystyle\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq|I|^{1/2}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}(I;B^{0}_{\infty,1})}^{1/2}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{2}_{\infty,1})}^{1/2}\leq C|I|^{1/2}X_{I},

(7.51)

\displaystyle\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{1/2}_{\infty,1})}

\displaystyle\leq|I|^{3/4}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}(I;B^{0}_{\infty,1})}^{3/4}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{2}_{\infty,1})}^{1/4}\leq C|I|^{3/4}X_{I}.

Summing (
7.47
) and
(
7.48
), then using
(
7.49
)–
(
7.51
) and the uniform critical estimate,
we obtain for
|I|\leq 1

\displaystyle X_{I}\leq{}

\displaystyle C(1+|I|)\left(\|\tau_{N}^{a}(t_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\int_{I}\|u_{N}^{a}(s)\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\,ds\right)

(7.52)

\displaystyle+C\delta(1+|I|)\big(|I|+|I|^{1/2}+|I|^{3/4}\big)X_{I}.

Choose a fixed
h_{0}\in(0,1]
so that the last coefficient is at most

1/2
whenever
|I|\leq h_{0}
. This choice is independent of
a
and
N
.
After absorption, iterate (
7.52
) over at most

1+T/h_{0}
intervals. Since

\sup_{a,N}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}
,
this proves, without assuming the parabolic norm in advance,

(7.53)

\sup_{a\in[0,1],\,N\geq 1}\left(\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}_{T}B^{2}_{\infty,1}}\right)\leq C_{T}\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Fix now
0<\eta<1
. Real interpolation of the two norms in
(
7.53
) gives

(7.54)

\sup_{a\in[0,1],\,N\geq 1}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{q_{\eta}}_{T}B^{1+\eta}_{\infty,1}}\leq C_{T}\mathcal{E}_{0},\qquad q_{\eta}=\frac{2}{1+\eta}>1.

In particular, for every interval
I\subset[0,T]
,

(7.55)

\sup_{a,N}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{1+\eta}_{\infty,1})}\leq C_{T}\mathcal{E}_{0}|I|^{(1-\eta)/2}.

We spell out the endpoint which otherwise causes a high–high to low
ambiguity. Bony’s decomposition and
\eta>0
give

(7.56)

\displaystyle\|u^{a}\cdot\nabla\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q(\nabla u^{a},\Theta_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}},

(7.57)

\displaystyle\|W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|Q(\nabla W_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\eta}\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1+\eta}_{\infty,1}}.

Let
I=[t_{0},t_{1}]\subset[0,T]
and set

\displaystyle V_{I}

\displaystyle=\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(I;B^{0}_{\infty,1})},

\displaystyle S_{I}

\displaystyle=\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{\widetilde{L}^{\infty}(I;B^{-1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}.

For clarity, the velocity estimate at index zero does not use an algebra
property of
B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. Since
\operatorname{div}W_{N}^{a}=0
, the term

W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla u_{N}^{a}
has the corresponding divergence form

\operatorname{div}(W_{N}^{a}\otimes u_{N}^{a})
(with the tensor convention of the
transport term), and Lemma
2.5
gives

(7.58)

\|W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}.

Thus the required positive derivative is supplied by the

B^{1}_{\infty,1}
factor and retained through the divergence/transport
structure; one never estimates a product of two bare
B^{0}_{\infty,1}

factors. The periodic Leray projector and its pressure multiplier are
bounded at index zero, while

\|\operatorname{div}\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}
.
Applying the transport estimate to this velocity equation and the heat
maximal-regularity estimate to the stress equation, and then using
(
7.56
)–
(
7.57
), yields

(7.59)

\displaystyle V_{I}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}\big(\|W_{N}^{a}(t_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+S_{I}\big),

(7.60)

\displaystyle S_{I}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\Theta_{N}^{a}(t_{0})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+C\left(|I|+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(I;B^{1+\eta}_{\infty,1})}\right)V_{I}+C\delta S_{I}.

Here
2D(W_{N}^{a})
contributes
C|I|V_{I}
, the damping term has the
favorable sign, and the terms transported by
u^{a}
are included in

C\delta S_{I}
. Choose first the universal smallness constant so that

C\delta\leq 1/4
. By
(
7.55
), one can then choose a length

0<h_{T}\leq\min\{1,h_{0}\}
, independent of
a
and
N
, such that the coefficient of
V_{I}
in
(
7.60
) is small whenever
|I|\leq h_{T}
.
Substitution into (
7.59
) and absorption give

V_{I}+S_{I}\leq C_{T}\big(\|W_{N}^{a}(t_{0})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}(t_{0})\|_{B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}\big).

Iterating over at most
1+T/h_{T}
intervals and using
(
7.45
) proves

(7.61)

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\big(

\displaystyle\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}_{T}B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\big)\leq C_{T}2^{-N}\varepsilon_{N}.

All constants are independent of
a
and
N
.

At the
H^{1}
level, the only apparently nonuniform factors are smooth norms
of
z_{N}^{a}
. We first establish their precise
2^{N}
growth. Applying the
dyadic transport estimate at velocity index
2
and the parabolic estimate
at stress index
1
gives, with

Z_{N}^{a}(t)=\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;B^{2}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}(0,t;B^{1}_{\infty,1})}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{1}(0,t;B^{3}_{\infty,1})},

the tame inequality

\displaystyle Z_{N}^{a}(t)

\displaystyle\leq C\big(\|S_{N}u_{0}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}+\|S_{N}\tau_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\big)

(7.62)

\displaystyle\quad+C\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big)\big(\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\big)\,ds.

There is no hidden quadratic dependence on
Z_{N}^{a}
. Indeed, the
commutators and the differentiated
Q
term obey

\displaystyle\sum_{j}2^{2j}\|[\Delta_{j},u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla]u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}},

\displaystyle\sum_{j}2^{j}\|[\Delta_{j},u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|Q(\nabla u_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+C\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}.

The pressure causes no additional loss. Taking the divergence of the
velocity equation and using
\operatorname{div}u_{N}^{a}=0
gives

-\Delta P_{N}^{a}=\operatorname{div}\operatorname{div}(u_{N}^{a}\otimes u_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a}),

and the periodic multiplier and tame product estimates imply

\|\nabla P_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{3}_{\infty,1}}+C\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}.

Thus the pressure also contains only the parabolic high norm or a controlled
critical factor times one velocity high norm.
In dimension three, the equivalent estimate for the good unknown contains
the vortex stretching and the multiplier commutator; their tame forms are

\displaystyle\|\omega_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\omega_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}+C\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|\omega_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}},

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},u_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}+C\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}.

Thus the three-dimensional terms also consist of a controlled critical
factor times one high norm. Since the cut-off initial data satisfy

\|S_{N}u_{0}\|_{B^{2}_{\infty,1}}+\|S_{N}\tau_{0}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C2^{N}\mathcal{E}_{0},

the uniform critical estimate and Gronwall’s inequality give

(7.63)

\sup_{a\in[0,1]}Z_{N}^{a}(T)\leq C_{T}2^{N}\mathcal{E}_{0},

where
C_{T}
is independent of
a
and
N
.

We now subtract the systems for
z^{a}
and
z_{N}^{a}
and test the velocity
equation by
-\Delta W_{N}^{a}
and the stress equation by

-\frac{1}{2}\Delta\Theta_{N}^{a}
. The differentiated linear coupling terms
cancel. The transport terms with advecting velocity
u^{a}
are
bounded by

C\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\big(\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\nabla\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big).

For the terms in which the difference is the advecting velocity, integration
by parts and Young’s inequality give, for example,

\displaystyle|\langle W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla u_{N}^{a},\Delta W_{N}^{a}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+C\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla^{2}u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}},

whereas the three non-transport terms in the stress equation satisfy

\displaystyle\big|\langle W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}+Q(\nabla u^{a},\Theta_{N}^{a})+Q(\nabla W_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a}),\Delta\Theta_{N}^{a}\rangle\big|

\displaystyle\quad\leq\frac{1}{4}\|\nabla^{2}\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}+C\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla u^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+C\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}.

In particular, (
7.63
) and the Besov embeddings yield

\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\big(\|\nabla^{2}u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}+\|\nabla\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}L^{\infty}}\big)\leq C_{T}2^{N}\mathcal{E}_{0}.

All the other coefficients in the preceding inequalities are bounded
uniformly by (
7.8
). Consequently, if

Y_{N}^{a}=\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\|\nabla\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2},

then

\frac{d}{dt}Y_{N}^{a}+c\|\nabla^{2}\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\leq B_{N}^{a}(t)Y_{N}^{a}+C_{T}2^{2N}\big(\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\big),\qquad\int_{0}^{T}B_{N}^{a}(t)\,dt\leq C_{T},

with both bounds uniform in
a
and
N
. Since

Y_{N}^{a}(0)\leq C\varepsilon_{N}^{2}
, the
2^{-N}
gain in
(
7.46
) and Gronwall’s inequality imply

(7.64)

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\big(

\displaystyle\|z^{a}-z_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\dot{H}^{1}\times\dot{H}^{1})}+\|\nabla^{2}\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}}\big)\leq C_{T}\varepsilon_{N}.

Combining this with (
7.46
), we shall use
below the uniform estimate

(7.65)

\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\|\nabla\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1})}\leq C_{T}\varepsilon_{N}.

We next recover the endpoint stress norm by an explicit frequency split.
Put
\beta_{2}=1/2
,
\beta_{3}=1/4
. The blockwise heat estimates
(
7.27
)–(
7.28
), applied to both stresses, imply
that for every
J<N-C
,

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}2^{J}\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{-1}_{\infty,1}}

(7.66)

\displaystyle\quad+C\sum_{j>J-C}\|\Delta_{j}\tau_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\mathcal{E}_{0}\,2^{-\beta_{d}J}.

The first term is the low-frequency part of the difference. The second is
the high-frequency tail of the original initial stress; the truncated
initial stress has no modes above
N
. The last term is the summable
high-frequency Duhamel remainder. Taking
J=\lfloor N/2\rfloor
and using
(
7.61
), define

(7.67)

r_{N}=C_{T}2^{J-N}\varepsilon_{N}+C\sum_{j>J-C}\|\Delta_{j}\tau_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}}+C\mathcal{E}_{0}\,2^{-\beta_{d}J}.

Then
r_{N}\to 0
and

\sup_{a}\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq r_{N}
.

Finally set
H_{N}^{a}=\Gamma^{a}-\Gamma_{N}^{a}
and

G_{N}=\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\|H_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}
.
The difference equation used below is

\displaystyle\partial_{t}H_{N}^{a}+u^{a}\cdot\nabla H_{N}^{a}+H_{N}^{a}={}

\displaystyle-W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{N}^{a}+(a-1)\mathcal{R}\Theta_{N}^{a}

\displaystyle+[\mathcal{R},u^{a}\cdot\nabla]\Theta_{N}^{a}+[\mathcal{R},W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{a}

(7.68)

\displaystyle+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u^{a},\Theta_{N}^{a})+\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla W_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})+\mathcal{S}_{N}^{a},

where the stretching difference is written without an implicit
dimension-dependent indicator:

\mathcal{S}_{N}^{a}=\begin{cases}0,&d=2,\\
(\omega^{a}-\omega_{N}^{a})\cdot\nabla u^{a}+\omega_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla W_{N}^{a},&d=3.\end{cases}

Notice also that

\|H_{N}^{a}(0)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\varepsilon_{N}
. To estimate the
right-hand side of (
7.68
), define

\Lambda_{N}^{a}(t)=\|H_{N}^{a}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}(t)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|(W_{N}^{a},\Theta_{N}^{a})(t)\|_{L^{2}}.

The periodic Biot–Savart estimate and

\omega^{a}-\omega_{N}^{a}=H_{N}^{a}+\mathcal{R}\Theta_{N}^{a}
imply

(7.69)

\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\omega^{a}-\omega_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\Lambda_{N}^{a}.

We now estimate the sources separately. Write

K_{t}(s)=e^{-c(t-s)}\mathbf{1}_{[0,t]}(s)
, where
c>0
is the constant in
the damped transport estimate. First, the transport structure and the
one-higher-order estimate for the truncated solution give

(7.70)

\displaystyle\sup_{t\leq T}\int_{0}^{t}K_{t}(s)\|W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla\Gamma_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\,ds

\displaystyle\leq C_{T}2^{N}\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}.

Next, (
7.32
),
|a-1|\leq 1
, and the uniform small
critical bounds yield, pointwise in time,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|[\mathcal{R},u^{a}\cdot\nabla]\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

(7.71)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C\big(\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}\big),

(7.72)

\displaystyle\|[\mathcal{R},W_{N}^{a}\cdot\nabla]\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\delta\Lambda_{N}^{a}.

It remains important not to use an algebra estimate at the endpoint

B^{0}_{\infty,1}
. Fix
0<\kappa<2-d/2
. The product law in
Lemma
2.5
, the low-frequency part of the multiplier estimate, and
(
7.33
) give

(7.73)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla u^{a},\Theta_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\delta\big(

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}+\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\infty,\infty}}\big).

Here the positive Besov norm is precisely where the dissipative part of the

H^{1}
difference estimate is needed. Indeed,

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\sup_{t\leq T}\int_{0}^{t}K_{t}(s)\|\Theta_{N}^{a}(s)\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\infty,\infty}}\,ds

(7.74)

\displaystyle\quad\leq C\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\left(\|\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}H^{1}}+\|\nabla^{2}\Theta_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}_{T}L^{2}}\right)\leq C_{T}\varepsilon_{N},

where Cauchy–Schwarz in time was used in the second line.

For the other quadratic term, the same product law gives

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla W_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{2}}\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}}

(7.75)

\displaystyle+C\|\nabla W_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big(\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\tau_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\infty,\infty}}\big).

The global energy estimate for the truncated solutions and
(
7.33
) imply, uniformly in
a
and
N
,

(7.76)

\sup_{t\leq T}\int_{0}^{t}K_{t}(s)\|\tau_{N}^{a}(s)\|_{B^{\kappa}_{\infty,\infty}}\,ds\leq C\mathcal{E}_{0}.

Combining (
7.69
),
(
7.64
), and
(
7.76
) in
(
7.75
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\sup_{t\leq T}\int_{0}^{t}K_{t}(s)\|\mathcal{R}Q(\nabla W_{N}^{a},\tau_{N}^{a})(s)\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\,ds

(7.77)

\displaystyle\qquad\leq C_{T}\delta\varepsilon_{N}+C\delta(G_{N}+r_{N}).

Finally, in dimension three, the divergence-free product estimate used in
(
7.36
) and (
7.69
) give

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{S}_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\omega^{a}-\omega_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|u^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}

(7.78)

\displaystyle\quad+C\|\omega_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\|W_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{1}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\delta\Lambda_{N}^{a}.

Applying the damped transport estimate to
(
7.68
) and using
(
7.70
)–
(
7.78
) therefore yields

(7.79)

\displaystyle G_{N}\leq C_{T}\Big(

\displaystyle\varepsilon_{N}+2^{N}\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\|u^{a}-u_{N}^{a}\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+r_{N}\Big)+C\delta G_{N}.

The factor
2^{N}
is canceled by the
2^{-N}
in
(
7.61
). Choosing the smallness constant so that

C\delta<1/2
proves

(7.80)

G_{N}\leq C_{T}(\varepsilon_{N}+r_{N}).

Using the periodic Biot–Savart estimate

\|\nabla(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\leq C\big(\|H_{N}^{a}\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}+\|\mathcal{R}(\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{B^{0}_{\infty,1}}\big),

we conclude that, for

c_{N}=C_{T}(\varepsilon_{N}+r_{N})\to 0
,

(7.81)

\sup_{a\in[0,1]}\|(u^{a}-u_{N}^{a},\tau^{a}-\tau_{N}^{a})\|_{L^{\infty}_{T}(\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{1}_{\infty,1})\times L^{\infty}_{T}(\dot{H}^{1}\cap B^{0}_{\infty,1})}\leq c_{N}.

For fixed
N
, the smooth norms of
z_{N}^{a}
on
[0,T]
are bounded by a
constant
C_{T,N}
uniformly in
a
. We include the derivative-loss
argument in the smooth middle difference. Put

\mathcal{U}_{N}=u_{N}^{a}-u_{N}^{0}
and

\mathcal{T}_{N}=\tau_{N}^{a}-\tau_{N}^{0}
, and choose an integer

s>d/2+1
. After applying derivatives of order at most
s
, the linear
coupling still cancels between the velocity energy and one half of the
stress energy. The apparently dangerous term is

Q(\nabla\mathcal{U}_{N},\tau_{N}^{0})
. Since
Q
has constant coefficients and
is bilinear, for suitable bilinear maps
\mathcal{B}_{\ell}
one has

Q(\nabla\mathcal{U}_{N},\tau_{N}^{0})=\sum_{\ell}\partial_{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{N},\tau_{N}^{0})-\sum_{\ell}\mathcal{B}_{\ell}(\mathcal{U}_{N},\partial_{\ell}\tau_{N}^{0}).

Integrating the first sum by parts places its derivative on

\mathcal{T}_{N}
